#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
impl Engine {
    pub fn new(store: Store, schema: Schema, programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::new_at(store, schema, programs, crate::now_ms())
    }

    /// Activate using the owning host's clock for parent liveness and captures.
    pub fn new_at(mut store: Store, mut schema: Schema, programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>, now: i64) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        columns::validate_before_prepare(&schema)?;
        schema.check_row_view_audiences()?;
        schema.prepare_contribution_indexes();
        schema.prepare_delivery_lookup();
        let issues = crate::schema::validate(&schema);
        if let Some(issue) = issues.first() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}: {}", issue.path, issue.message)));
        }
        let programs = programs.into_iter().map(|p| (p.name.clone(), p)).collect();
        let (generation, metadata) = Self::activate(&mut store, &schema, &programs, None, now, true, None, None)?;
        let dev_effects = metadata.dev_effects;
        store.exclude_ephemeral(&schema);
        Ok(Self { timeline_schedule: timeline::Schedule::default(), ephemeral: std::sync::Arc::new(ephemeral::Hub::new(metadata.store_id, generation, &schema)), store, schema, programs, generation, expiry_after: BTreeMap::new(), dev_effects, ceilings: Ceilings::default(), entropy: crate::ids::seed(), minter: crate::ids::Mint::default(), #[cfg(feature = "native")] native: None, #[cfg(feature = "native")] native_logs: native::Logs::default(), presence: None })
    }

    /// A detached read owner for HTTP query/sync computation. Creating it is
    /// brief; materializing its response does not hold the serving engine.
    pub fn reader(&self) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Ok(Self { timeline_schedule: timeline::Schedule::default(), ephemeral: self.ephemeral.clone(), store: self.store.reader()?, schema: self.schema.clone(), programs: self.programs.clone(),
            ceilings: self.ceilings, entropy: 0, minter: self.minter.clone(), generation: self.generation, expiry_after: BTreeMap::new(), dev_effects: self.dev_effects,
            #[cfg(feature = "native")] native: self.native.clone(),
            #[cfg(feature = "native")] native_logs: native::Logs::default(), presence: self.presence.clone() })
    }

    /// The registry every program's `connected[p]` reads, on this engine.
    pub fn set_presence(&mut self, presence: Option<std::sync::Arc<dyn Presence>>) {
        self.presence = presence;
    }


    /// Attach to an already activated semantic generation without installing
    /// caller source. Reads of its identity and compiled content are one snapshot.
    pub fn open(store: Store) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::open_at(store, crate::now_ms())
    }

    pub fn open_at(store: Store, now: i64) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::open_selected_at(store, now, None)
    }

    fn open_selected_at(mut store: Store, now: i64, expected_release: Option<u64>) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = store.opening_transaction()?;
        Store::check_selected_release(tx.conn(), expected_release)?;
        let (generation, schema, programs): (i64, String, String) = tx.conn().query_row(
            "SELECT g.generation, g.schema, g.programs FROM _generations g JOIN _meta m ON m.key = 'generation' AND g.generation = CAST(m.value AS INTEGER)",
            [], |row| Ok((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?, row.get(2)?)))
            .map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(format!("no readable activated backend in this store: {e}")))?;
//! The same fixture loader serves query testing and residency at either CLI entry point.
use super::Options;
use crate::project::Project;
use snapback4_core::{Engine, wire};
use snapback4_lang::Bound;

pub(super) fn engine(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, options: &Options) -> Result<Engine, String> {
    engine_at(project, bound, options, snapback4_core::now_ms())
}

pub(super) fn engine_at(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, options: &Options, now: i64) -> Result<Engine, String> {
    let Some(fixture) = options.flags.get("fixture") else {
        if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
            let store = snapback4_core::Store::memory().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            return Engine::new_at(store, bound.schema.clone(), bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|e| e.to_string());
        }
        return project.open_engine(bound).map_err(|error| format!("cannot open the store: {error}"));
    };
    let started = std::time::Instant::now();
    let mut schema = bound.schema.clone();
    // Residency defers ordinary maintains until after bulk loading and builds
    // them once; column derives stay installed and maintain as rows insert.
    let residency = options.flags.contains_key("residency");
    if residency { schema.maintains.retain(|m| m.private.is_some()); }
    let store = snapback4_core::Store::memory().map_err(|error| format!("cannot open a store: {error}"))?;
    let mut engine = Engine::new_at(store, schema, bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|error| format!("cannot open a store: {error}"))?;
    let mut names = Vec::new();
    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(fixture).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture directory {fixture}: {error}"))? {
        let entry = entry.map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture directory {fixture}: {error}"))?;
        names.push(entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string());
    }
    names.sort();
    // One batch for the whole directory: the engine orders parents before
    // children within a batch, so file order (and read_dir order) cannot make
    // a read-through child or a reference miss its parent.
    let mut rows = Vec::new();
    let mut unknown = Vec::new();
    for name in names {
        let Some(table) = name.strip_suffix(".jsonl") else { continue };
        if name == super::test_cases::FILE { continue; }
        let path = std::path::Path::new(fixture).join(&name);
        let Some(definition) = bound.schema.tables.get(table) else {
            if options.flags.contains_key("saved-fixture-fallback") {
                eprintln!("test: skipped {} — saved source declares no table {table}; pass --fixture <dir> to select contemporary fixtures explicitly", path.display());
                continue;
            }
            // @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — the starter's own unmodified sample is skipped loudly; a project's file for a missing table still refuses.
            if !options.flags.contains_key("source") && super::init::starter_fixture(&name).is_some_and(|starter| std::fs::read_to_string(&path).is_ok_and(|text| text == starter)) {
                eprintln!("test: WARNING: skipped {} — the todo starter's unmodified sample rows for a `{table}` table your snapback/*.q does not declare; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it and the starter's other leftovers", path.display());
                continue;
            }
            unknown.push(name);
            continue;
        };
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture file {}: {error}", path.display()))?;
        for (line_number, line) in text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_, line)| !line.trim().is_empty()) {
            let at = format!("{name}:{}", line_number + 1);
            let mut json: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(line).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}"))?;
            // A residency fixture is often an exported store: engine-maintained
            // columns are dropped and rebuilt from the source rows. `test`
            // refuses them as an insert and `/seed` do (E_MAINTAINED_COLUMN).
            if residency && let Some(fields) = json.as_object_mut() { fields.retain(|field, _| !definition.maintained.contains_key(field)); }
            relative_times(definition, &mut json, now).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
            if let Some(why) = wire::bare_principal(definition, &json) { return Err(format!("{at}: {why}")); }
            let row = wire::row_from_json(definition, &json).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}\n  hint: a fixture row is one JSON object per line with the table's fields and an \"id\"; see snapback4 guide testing"))?;
            rows.push((table.to_owned(), row));
        }
    }
    // A misspelled file name would otherwise load nothing and still pass.
    if !unknown.is_empty() {
        let tables: Vec<&str> = bound.schema.visible_tables().map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).collect();
        // 0.4.17's init wrote the todo starter's rows beside a project's own schema.
        let starter = unknown.iter().any(|name| name == "todos.jsonl")
            && std::fs::read_to_string(std::path::Path::new(fixture).join("todos.jsonl")).is_ok_and(|text| text.contains("\"id\":\"todo-milk\""));
        let starter = if starter { format!("\n  hint: todos.jsonl is the todo starter's sample that `snapback4 init` wrote; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it and the starter's {} while they are unmodified (or delete them, and the starter's list/add/toggle/remove lines, yourself)", super::test_cases::FILE) } else { String::new() };
        return Err(format!("{fixture}: {} name{} no table in snapback/*.q; nothing was run{starter}\n  hint: a fixture file is <table>.jsonl for a declared table ({}), plus {}; see snapback4 guide testing",
            unknown.join(", "), if unknown.len() == 1 { "s" } else { "" }, tables.join(", "), super::test_cases::FILE));
    }
    // Fixture rows take `/seed`'s insert path (defaults, sequences, unique with the author's codes, caps, maintained views), not the trusted bulk import.
    let loaded = engine.seed_at(rows, now).map_err(|error| {
        let text = error.to_string();
        let hint = if text.contains("NOT_FOUND") { "\n  hint: every fixture file loads as one batch, parents first; NOT_FOUND means a referenced or read-through parent row is in no fixture file (check the id)" } else { "" };
        format!("{fixture}: {text}{hint}")
    })?;
    if residency {
        engine.adopt_at(bound.schema.clone(), bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|error| format!("cannot build fixture maintains: {error}"))?;
    }
    if !options.json { println!("test: loaded {loaded} rows from {fixture} in {:.1} s", started.elapsed().as_secs_f64()); }
    Ok(engine)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#5-fixtures-and-dev-evidence — relative time leaves, seeds and operations share one captured clock.
/// Only typed time leaves interpret `now`: text and JSON retain their literals.
pub(crate) fn relative_times(table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table, row: &mut serde_json::Value, now: i64) -> Result<(), String> {

//! Isolated complete fixtures, including captured authority and asset ownership.
use super::*;
impl Store {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#112-reproducible-campaigns-and-reachability — fork committed state, never reinterpret stored rows as new seed input.
    pub fn fork_memory(&self) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let mut fork = Self::memory()?;
        {
            let backup = rusqlite::backup::Backup::new(&self.conn, &mut fork.conn)?;
            backup.run_to_completion(256, std::time::Duration::ZERO, None)?;
        }
        fork.catalog = self.catalog.clone();
        fork.identity = self.identity.clone();
        fork.server = self.server;
        if self.assets_dir.exists() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(&fork.assets_dir).map_err(io)?;
            for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&self.assets_dir).map_err(io)? {
                let entry = entry.map_err(io)?;
                if !entry.file_type().map_err(io)?.is_file() {
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Store("fixture asset ledger contains a non-file".into()));
                }
                std::fs::copy(entry.path(), fork.assets_dir.join(entry.file_name())).map_err(io)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(fork)
    }
}
//! The SQLite store. Two generic tables carry every application table: `r`
//! holds row images by (table number, id) and `i` holds every index entry as
//! one order-preserving key (`key.rs`) so a prefix scan, a point lookup and
//! a cursor position are all one B-tree range. The rest is the engine's own
//! bookkeeping: the catalog of table and index numbers, commits, the
//! partition log, applied operation ids, principals, sessions, generations.

#[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
#[path = "store/selected.rs"]
mod selected;

pub use crate::rows::{Bounds, Dir, index_key, index_keys, index_values};
use crate::schema::{Index, Schema, Table};
use crate::value::{Value, canonical_json};
use rusqlite::{Connection, OptionalExtension, params};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

mod fork;
mod activation;
mod activation_cache;
mod activation_index;
mod activation_plan;
mod activation_scan;

mod live_activation;

mod adoption_tests;

#[cfg(test)]
mod dormant_tests;

const STORE_VERSION: &str = "snapback4-store-1";

#[cfg(unix)]
fn file_identity(conn: &Connection) -> Result<Option<(u64, u64)>, RuntimeError> {
    use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;
    conn.path().filter(|path| !path.is_empty()).map(|path| {
        std::fs::metadata(path).map(|m| (m.dev(), m.ino()))
            .map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(format!("database identity at {path}: {e}")))
    }).transpose()
}

pub struct Store {
    conn: Connection,
    /// Server stores own timed publication. Device stores receive only rows
    /// the server has already published and never consult this private ledger.
    server: bool,
    // Fields drop in declaration order: retain the sidecar ownership lock until
    // SQLite has closed. No raw descriptor to the database is held here.
    ownership: Option<std::sync::Arc<std::fs::File>>,
    #[cfg(unix)]
    file_identity: Option<(u64, u64)>,
    page_size: u64,
    catalog: BTreeMap<String, i64>,
    /// Where uploaded bytes live: `assets/` beside the database file, or a
    /// directory that dies with the store for a store in memory.
    assets_dir: PathBuf,
    /// A store in memory owns its assets directory: nothing creates it until
    /// the first upload, and dropping the store removes it. Before this, every
    /// `Store::memory()` created one and nothing removed it — one empty
    /// directory per in-memory engine, four million of them in a week of test
    /// runs on one machine, until listing the temp directory took minutes.
    ephemeral_assets: bool,
    identity: activation::Identity,
    #[cfg(test)]
    full_adoption: bool,
    pub adoption_report: adoption_report::AdoptionReport,
}

impl Drop for Store {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if self.ephemeral_assets {
            let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.assets_dir);
        }
    }
}

/// One transaction over the store, with the catalog in reach.
pub struct Tx<'a> {
    activation_scan: Option<activation_scan::Buffer>,
    pub changes: std::cell::RefCell<crate::revocation::Recorder>,
    sequence_table: std::cell::Cell<Option<bool>>,
    tx: rusqlite::Transaction<'a>,
    page_size: u64,
    server: bool,
    catalog: &'a BTreeMap<String, i64>,
}

fn scan_sql(dir: Dir) -> &'static str {
    match dir {
        Dir::Asc => {
            "SELECT r.data, i.k FROM i JOIN r ON r.t = ?1 AND r.id = i.id WHERE i.ti = ?2 AND i.k >= ?3 AND i.k < ?4 ORDER BY i.k ASC LIMIT ?5"
        }
        Dir::Desc => {
            "SELECT r.data, i.k FROM i JOIN r ON r.t = ?1 AND r.id = i.id WHERE i.ti = ?2 AND i.k >= ?3 AND i.k < ?4 ORDER BY i.k DESC LIMIT ?5"
        }
    }
}

impl Store {
    /// Inspect a dev store without creating a database, changing permissions, or
    /// running the writable store's initialization and upgrade path.
    pub fn open_read_only(path: &Path) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let mut conn = Connection::open_with_flags(path, rusqlite::OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_READ_ONLY)?;
        #[cfg(unix)]
        let file_identity = file_identity(&conn)?;
        conn.busy_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))?;
        let tx = conn.transaction()?;
        let version: String = tx.query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'version'", [], |row| {
                row.get(0)
            })?;
        if version != STORE_VERSION && version != dormant::VERSION && version != "snapback4-release-store-2" { return Err(RuntimeError::Store(format!("store version {version} is not {STORE_VERSION}"))); }
        let catalog = tx.prepare("SELECT name, num FROM _catalog")?.query_map([], |row| Ok((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
        let identity = activation::identity(&tx)?;
        tx.rollback()?;
        let real = std::fs::canonicalize(path).unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_path_buf());
        let assets_dir = real.parent().unwrap_or(Path::new(".")).join("assets");
        let page_size = conn.query_row("PRAGMA page_size", [], |row| row.get::<_, i64>(0))? as u64;
        Ok(Self { #[cfg(test)] full_adoption: false, adoption_report: Default::default(), conn,
            server: false, ownership: None, #[cfg(unix)] file_identity, page_size, catalog, identity, assets_dir, ephemeral_assets: false })
    }

    /// Classify against one read snapshot of the active generation and rows.
    pub fn successor(&mut self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<Vec<crate::successor::Step>, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.read_transaction()?;
        let steps = successor::assess(&tx, schema)?;
        tx.rollback()?;
        Ok(steps)
    }

    /// Preview the same atomic launch admitted by dev, without changing the store.
    pub fn check_timeline_activation(&mut self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<Vec<timeline_launch::Estimate>, RuntimeError> {
        self.check_timeline_activation_at(schema, crate::now_ms(), true)
    }

    pub fn check_timeline_activation_at(&mut self, schema: &Schema, now: i64, allow_launch: bool) -> Result<Vec<timeline_launch::Estimate>, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.read_transaction()?;
        let estimates = timeline_launch::preflight(&tx, schema, now, allow_launch)?;
        tx.rollback()?;
        Ok(estimates)
    }

    pub fn open(path: &Path) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::open_with_busy_timeout(path, std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
    }

    /// A caller with its own pre-admission deadline can own the lock wait.
    // @ref LLP 3000.011.004.000#2 — only open/adoption retries use a caller-owned deadline.
    pub fn open_with_busy_timeout(path: &Path, timeout: std::time::Duration) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::open_base(path, timeout, true)
    }

    // Shared server/device initialization; server evidence is added only by
    // the server entry points, never when opening an older native device.
    fn open_base(path: &Path, timeout: std::time::Duration, server: bool) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let io = |e: std::io::Error| RuntimeError::Store(format!("private store: {e}"));
        // Release links intentionally point at the one shared database.
        let real = std::fs::canonicalize(path).unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_path_buf());
        if let Some(parent) = real.parent().filter(|p| !p.as_os_str().is_empty()) {
            // A store's parent is its data root (assets live beside it), so
            // opening an existing store tightens that root as well.
            crate::private_fs::directory(parent).map_err(io)?;
        }
        // SQLite's Unix VFS copies the database mode when creating WAL,
        // journal and shared-memory files. Set it before opening SQLite;
        // chmod only after preparation would leave a disclosure window.
        crate::private_fs::database_file(&real).map_err(io)?;
        for suffix in ["-wal", "-shm", "-journal"] {
            crate::private_fs::existing_file(&PathBuf::from(format!("{}{suffix}", real.display())))
                .map_err(io)?;
        }
        let conn = Connection::open(&real)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.033#1.3 — even dropping a refused writable connection
        // must preserve committed WAL frames instead of checkpointing them.
        conn.set_db_config(rusqlite::config::DbConfig::SQLITE_DBCONFIG_NO_CKPT_ON_CLOSE, true)?;
        conn.busy_timeout(timeout)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.013#4-following-without-caps — published watermarks must survive a power loss.
        // Delivery publishes partition watermarks as well as replayable copies.
        // Losing an acknowledged background commit could rewind a device's
        // watermark, so requests and maintenance share the durable policy.
        // A refused open must preserve even a legacy rollback-journal file's
        // header. New stores choose WAL; existing stores retain their mode.
        if conn.query_row("PRAGMA page_count", [], |r| r.get::<_, i64>(0))? == 0 {
            conn.execute_batch("PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;")?;
        }
        conn.execute_batch("PRAGMA synchronous = FULL; PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF;")?;
        // A release serves the shared store through a link; the assets sit
        // beside the file itself, so every release serves the same bytes.
        let real = std::fs::canonicalize(path).unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_path_buf());
        let assets_dir = real.parent().map(|dir| dir.join("assets")).unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from("assets"));
        let store = Self::prepare(conn, assets_dir, false, server)?;
        for suffix in ["-wal", "-shm", "-journal"] {
            crate::private_fs::existing_file(&PathBuf::from(format!("{}{suffix}", real.display())))
                .map_err(io)?;
        }
        Ok(store)
    }

    pub fn memory() -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let assets_dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
//! Field writes use the old owner; row transition rules still constrain them.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — ownership grants only the explicitly touched owned fields.
use super::runner::Runner;
use super::rules::{self, Probe};
use crate::{error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, schema::{Effect, RuleExpr}, value::Value};

/// Delivery accounting must not make another recipient's unknown membership
/// a dependency of the writer's prediction. Server witnesses remain exact.
struct DeliveryProbe<'a, 'b> {
    runner: &'a mut Runner<'b>,
    known: bool,
    allowance: &'a super::Authorization,
}

impl Probe for DeliveryProbe<'_, '_> {
    fn take_changes(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<(i64, super::rules::Verdict)>> { self.runner.witness_changes.take() }
    fn replace_expiry(&mut self, expiry: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> { self.runner.next_expiry.replace(expiry) }
    fn exists(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], equalities: &[(String, Value)]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if !self.runner.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, 1)? { return Ok(false); }
        let (present, known) = self.runner.probe_witness(self.allowance, table, index, key, equalities, false)?;
        self.known &= known;
        Ok(present)
    }

    fn created(&mut self, _table: &str, _index: &str, _key: &[Value]) -> bool { false }
}

impl<'r> Runner<'r> {
    pub(crate) fn field_owner_can_read(&mut self, rule: &RuleExpr, owner: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        // Recipient witnesses are exact but are not the caller's observations.
        // Partition accounting prepays their schema-derived probe/step bound.
        // A recipient's witness deadline never enters the writer's answer: the scope is dropped, not emitted.
        let delivery_expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let result = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(rule), &[], |runner, allowance| {
            let mut probe = DeliveryProbe { runner, known: true, allowance };
            let readable = rules::evaluate(rule, owner, None, row, false, &mut probe)?;
            Ok(readable && probe.known)
        });
        drop(delivery_expiry);
        result
    }

    pub(crate) fn prepay_field_delivery(&mut self, rule: &RuleExpr, slots: u64, maintained: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let probes = slots.saturating_mul(rules::rule_probes(rule));
        if maintained {
            self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes)?;
            self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::Steps, slots)?;
        } else {
            self.meter.charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes)?;
            self.meter.step(slots)?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    pub(super) fn authorize_fields(&self, table: &str, row: &Value, supplied: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let fields = supplied.as_object()?;
        let mut owned = false;
        let mut ordinary = false;
        for field in fields.keys().filter(|field| field.as_str() != "id") {
            if let Some(authority) = definition.written_by.get(field) {
                owned = true;
                if !self.context.is_system() && row.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone())) {
                    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
                    self.refusing_clause(table, &format!("field authority {field}"));
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_FIELD_AUTHORITY", message: format!("{table}.{field} is written by .{}; omit {field} or write as the principal in .{} (guide rules)", authority.owner, authority.owner) });
                }
            } else { ordinary = true; }
        }
        Ok(owned && !ordinary)
    }

    pub(super) fn authorize_update_fields(&mut self, table: &str, old: &Value, next: &Value, patch: &Value, readable: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let owned_only = self.authorize_fields(table, old, patch).map_err(|error| if readable { error } else { self.no_write_target() })?;
        // An unrelated owner's omitted field is unknown, not its literal
        // default. It prevents prediction only when this transition needs it,
        // or when changing ownership would expose it to this device.
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        if self.device && definition.written_by.iter().any(|(field, authority)|
            !authority.audience && old.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone()))
            && ((!owned_only || definition.update_transition) && definition.rules.update.needs_field_value(field, &definition.columns[field])
                || next.field(&authority.owner).ok() == Some(&Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone())))) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("{table}: this transition needs another principal's private field; queue it for the server (guide rules)")));
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — a named transition is not permission to test guesses against someone else's private value.
        if !self.context.is_system() && let Some((field, authority)) = definition.written_by.iter().find(|(field, authority)|
            !authority.audience && old.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone()))
            && definition.rules.update.needs_field_value(field, &definition.columns[*field])) {
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            self.refusing_clause(table, &format!("field authority {field}"));
            return Err(if readable { RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_FIELD_AUTHORITY", message: format!("{table}.{field}: this transition needs the private value written by .{}; write as that owner or declare readable by audience (guide rules)", authority.owner) } } else { self.no_write_target() });
        }
        let transition = self.schema.tables[table].update_transition;
        if (!owned_only || transition) && !self.check_rule(table, Effect::Update, Some(old), next)? {
            return Err(if readable { RuntimeError::Rule { table: table.into(), effect: Effect::Update } } else { self.no_write_target() });
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    pub(crate) fn disclosed_row<'a>(&self, definition: &'a crate::schema::Table, row: Value) -> crate::value::Disclosed<'a> {
        match self.system_access() {
            Some(system) => definition.system_fields(row, &system),
            None => definition.project_fields(row, Some(&self.context.viewer)),
        }
    }

    pub(crate) fn project_row(&mut self, table: &str, row: Value) -> Result<super::authorized_point::AuthorizedRow<'r>, RuntimeError> {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        // A merge may retain previously authorized results after its next lane
        // cuts. Revalidate their authority internally without treating that
        // existing logical cut as a denied row. Preserve the original cut.
        let cap = self.meter.capped_at.take();
        let result = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&definition.rules.read), &[], |runner, _| runner.authorize_image(table,row));
        if cap.is_some() { self.meter.capped_at = cap; }
        result?.ok_or_else(|| { self.refusing_clause(table, "read rule"); self.no_write_target() })
    }

    pub(super) fn private_scan_authority(&self, table: &str, uniform: bool, admitted: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        // A bounded hidden-row walk exposes population through both its meter
        // and its cut. Require a readable scope before walking protected rows.
        if !uniform && !admitted && self.schema.tables[table].written_by.values().any(|a| !a.audience) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_FIELD_AUTHORITY", message: format!("{table}: scope this read to a uniformly readable audience before scanning rows with private fields (guide rules)") });
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}
//! Field authority and projection share one schema fact on every host.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — the owner is a principal on this row, not a row update grant.
use super::*;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct FieldAuthority {
    pub owner: String,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")]
    pub audience: bool,
}

impl Table {
    pub fn private_field(&self, field: &str) -> bool {
        self.written_by.get(field).is_some_and(|authority| !authority.audience)
    }

    pub fn field_owners(&self, row: &Value) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        self.written_by.values().filter(|a| !a.audience).filter_map(|a|
            row.field(&a.owner).ok().and_then(|v| v.as_str().ok()).map(str::to_owned)).collect()
    }
}

// Private values cannot enter an index key (including cursors), an audience,
// attribution, or a materialized aggregate's ordinary readable output. This
// also checks resolved/inferred indexes and raw schemas, not just syntax.
pub fn validate(schema: &Schema) -> Vec<SchemaIssue> {
    let mut issues = Vec::new();
    let mut issue = |name: &str, field: &str, why: String| issues.push(SchemaIssue {
        path: format!("tables.{name}.columns.{field}"), message: format!("written by: {name}.{field} {why}") });
    for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
        for (field, authority) in &table.written_by {
            if !table.columns.contains_key(field) || field == "id" || table.columns.get(&authority.owner) != Some(&ColumnType::Principal)
                || table.written_by.contains_key(&authority.owner) || table.maintained.contains_key(field) || table.recorded.contains_key(field) {
                issue(name, field, format!("needs an ordinary required principal owner column .{} on the same table", authority.owner));
            }
            if !table.defaults.contains_key(field) {
                issue(name, field, "needs a declared default for inserts by other principals".into());
            }
            if authority.audience { continue; }
            if table.indexes.values().any(|index| index.uses_column(field)) || table.expire.as_ref() == Some(field) {
                issue(name, field, "is private and cannot supply an index/cursor or lifetime; declare readable by audience to use it there".into());
            }
            for timeline in &schema.timelines {
                let mut dependencies = BTreeSet::new();
                for mapping in [&timeline.authors[..], &timeline.posts[..], &timeline.reposts[..], &timeline.edges[..]] {
                    if mapping[0] == *name { dependencies.extend(mapping[1..].iter().cloned()); }
                }
                if timeline.posts[0] == *name && let Some(filter) = &timeline.filter { filter.fields(&mut dependencies); }
                if dependencies.contains(field) {
                    issue(name, field, "is private and cannot supply a timeline mapping or predicate; declare readable by audience first".into());
                }
            }
            for maintain in &schema.maintains {
                if let Some(key) = &maintain.row_key
                    && key.table == *name && key.fields().contains(field)
                    && !requires_owner(&table.rules.read, &authority.owner) {
                    issue(name, field, "is private and can feed a row-keyed count only when the member read rule requires its owner; restrict the member audience or declare readable by audience".into());
                }
                if maintain.source == *name && maintain.dependencies().contains(field) {
                    issue(name, field, "is private and cannot feed a readable aggregate; declare readable by audience first".into());
                }
            }
        }
        for (other_name, other) in &schema.tables {
            for capture in other.recorded.values() {
                if capture.table == *name && table.private_field(&capture.column) { issue(other_name, &capture.column, "cannot record another row's private field".into()); }
            }
        }
        for (effect, rule) in [("read", &table.rules.read), ("insert", &table.rules.insert), ("update", &table.rules.update), ("delete", &table.rules.delete)] {
            private_rule(schema, name, rule, effect == "update" && table.update_transition, &mut issue);
        }
    }
    issues
}

// Every admitted member reader must also be allowed to see the predicate input.
// A conjunctive owner grant proves that; an alternative grant does not.
fn requires_owner(rule: &RuleExpr, owner: &str) -> bool {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(field), RuleTerm::Viewer)
        | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(field)) => field == owner,
        RuleExpr::And(parts) => parts.iter().any(|part| requires_owner(part, owner)),
        _ => false,
    }
}

fn private_rule(schema: &Schema, name: &str, rule: &RuleExpr, update: bool, issue: &mut impl FnMut(&str, &str, String)) {
    let term = |term: &RuleTerm, issue: &mut dyn FnMut(&str, &str, String)| {
        if !update && let RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) = term
            && schema.tables[name].private_field(field) {
            issue(name, field, "is private; only a named update (old, next) transition may use it as a rule input; declare readable by audience for other rules".into());
        }
    };
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => { term(a,issue); term(b,issue); }
        RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => for item in items { private_rule(schema,name,item,update,issue); },
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => private_rule(schema,name,inner,update,issue),
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, key, equalities, .. } => {
            for item in key { term(item,issue); }
            for (field,value) in equalities {
                term(value,issue);
                if schema.table(table).is_some_and(|t|t.private_field(field)) { issue(table,field,"is private and cannot be probed from a rule".into()); }
            }
        }
        _ => {}
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    /// Comparing an untouched field with itself needs no knowledge of its
    /// value. Every other use (including a probe key) requires field visibility.
    pub fn needs_field_value(&self, field: &str, kind: &ColumnType) -> bool {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(name) | RuleTerm::OldField(name) | RuleTerm::NextField(name) if name == field);
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) => term(a) != term(b),
            // null is not ordered, even against itself. Its presence is private.
            Self::Compare(_,a,b) => (term(a) || term(b)) && (kind.is_optional() || term(a) != term(b)),
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|rule|rule.needs_field_value(field, kind)),
            Self::Not(inner) => inner.needs_field_value(field, kind),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,value)|value)).any(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }

    /// Fold only proven comparisons of an untouched, invisible field. This
    /// avoids interpreting an omitted device value as null or as its default.
    pub fn fold_untouched_field(&mut self, field: &str, kind: &ColumnType) {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(name) | RuleTerm::OldField(name) | RuleTerm::NextField(name) if name == field);
        let constant = match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) if term(a) && term(b) => Some(true),
            Self::Ne(a,b) if term(a) && term(b) => Some(false),
            Self::Compare(op,a,b) if !kind.is_optional() && term(a) && term(b) =>
                Some(matches!(op, crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gte | crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lte)),
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => {
                for item in items { item.fold_untouched_field(field,kind); }
                None
            }
            Self::Not(inner) => { inner.fold_untouched_field(field,kind); None }
            _ => None,
        };
        if let Some(value) = constant { *self = if value { Self::Allow } else { Self::Deny }; }
    }

    pub fn references_field(&self, field: &str) -> bool {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(name) | RuleTerm::OldField(name) | RuleTerm::NextField(name) if name == field);
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) | Self::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) || term(b),
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|rule| rule.references_field(field)),
            Self::Not(inner) => inner.references_field(field),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,value)|value)).any(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn ordered_optional_types_require_private_values() {
        for kind in [ColumnType::Int, ColumnType::Time, ColumnType::Decimal { scale: 2 },
            ColumnType::Money { currency: "USD".into(), scale: 2 }, ColumnType::Text { max: 20 }] {
            for op in [crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gte,crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gt,crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lte,crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lt] {
                let rule = RuleExpr::Compare(op,RuleTerm::NextField("secret".into()),RuleTerm::OldField("secret".into()));
                assert!(rule.needs_field_value("secret",&ColumnType::Optional(Box::new(kind.clone()))),"{kind:?}: {op:?}");
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn created_and_exists_terms_require_private_values() {
        for field in ["secret", "otherSecret"] {
            for term in [RuleTerm::Field(field.into()), RuleTerm::OldField(field.into()), RuleTerm::NextField(field.into())] {
                for equalities in [false,true] {
                    let key = if equalities { vec![] } else { vec![term.clone()] };
                    let predicates = if equalities { vec![("value".into(),term.clone())] } else { vec![] };
                    let exists = RuleExpr::Exists { table:"guesses".into(),index:"byValue".into(),provenance:crate::ir::KeyProvenance::Const,key:key.clone(),equalities:predicates.clone() };
                    let created = RuleExpr::Created { table:"guesses".into(),index:"byValue".into(),provenance:crate::ir::KeyProvenance::Const,key,equalities:predicates };
                    for rule in [exists,created] { assert!(rule.needs_field_value(field,&ColumnType::Optional(Box::new(ColumnType::Int)))); }
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

//! Reads: point lookups, bounded scans with the read rule hoisted where the
//! prefix decides it, opaque cursors, and merges of ordered lanes. Every
//! candidate row is charged to the meter; a query cut by the meter returns
//! what it found and is `capped` (LLP 2000.000 §6).

/// Row-image bytes one execution may step over as expired, unswept rows.
pub(super) const EXPIRED_STEP_GUARD: u64 = 256 << 20;
/// The internal scratch one stepped-over expired row reserves, beside its key.
const EXPIRED_STEP: u64 = 256;

// Only the read evaluator mints key evidence, from evaluated inputs, validated
// cursors, or authorized result positions. Storage images cannot call a raw
// scalar/key pricing API; row fields use AuthorizedRow::scalar_heap instead.
struct DisclosedKey<'a>(&'a Value);
struct DisclosedKeys<'a>(&'a [Value]);
struct DisclosedBounds<'a>(&'a Bounds);
impl DisclosedKey<'_> {
    fn heap(self) -> u64 {
        match self.0 {
            Value::String(s) | Value::Id(s) => word_heap::text(s),
            Value::Bytes(b) => word_heap::allocation(b.len()),
            Value::Array(a) => DisclosedKeys(a).heap(), _ => 0,
        }
    }
    fn copy_heap(self) -> u64 {
        match self.0 {
            Value::String(s) | Value::Id(s) => s.len() as u64,
            Value::Bytes(b) => b.len() as u64,
            Value::Array(a) => {
                word_heap::slots::<Value>(a.len()) + a.iter().map(|v|DisclosedKey(v).copy_heap()).sum::<u64>()
            }
            _ => 0,
        }
    }
}
impl DisclosedKeys<'_> {
    fn heap(self) -> u64 { word_heap::slots::<Value>(self.0.len()) + self.0.iter().map(|v|DisclosedKey(v).heap()).sum::<u64>() }
}
impl DisclosedBounds<'_> {
    fn heap(self) -> u64 {
        let b = self.0;
        [&b.after, &b.at_or_after, &b.before].into_iter().flatten().map(|v|DisclosedKeys(v).heap()).sum::<u64>()
            + [&b.gt, &b.gte, &b.lt, &b.lte].into_iter().flatten().map(|v|DisclosedKey(v).heap()).sum::<u64>()
            + b.word.as_ref().map_or(0, |s| word_heap::text(s))
    }
}

fn key_verdict_heap(index: &crate::schema::Index, prefix: &[Value], viewer: &str, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> u64 {
    // The prefix row's collect buffer, map, Arc, viewer and probe term clones.
    let fields = index.components.iter().zip(prefix).filter_map(|(c,v)| c.column().map(|name| (name, v)));
    let row = fields.map(|(name,v)| {
            word_heap::node::<String, Value>() + word_heap::text(name) + DisclosedKey(v).heap()
        }).sum::<u64>();
    word_heap::vec_growth::<(String, Value)>(prefix.len()) + row + word_heap::slots::<usize>(2) + word_heap::text(viewer) + rule_values(rule, prefix, viewer)
}
fn rule_values(rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr, prefix: &[Value], viewer: &str) -> u64 {
    use crate::schema::{RuleExpr::*, RuleTerm};
    let term = |t: &RuleTerm| match t {
        RuleTerm::Viewer => word_heap::text(viewer), RuleTerm::Literal(v) => DisclosedKey(v).heap(),
        RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } | RuleTerm::Field(_) | RuleTerm::OldField(_) | RuleTerm::NextField(_) => prefix.iter().map(|v|DisclosedKey(v).heap()).sum(),
    };
    match rule {
        Eq(a,b) | Ne(a,b) => term(a) + term(b),
        And(rs) | Or(rs) => rs.iter().map(|r| rule_values(r, prefix, viewer)).sum(), Not(r) => rule_values(r, prefix, viewer),
        Exists { table, key, equalities, .. } | Created { table, key, equalities, .. } => {
            word_heap::text(table)
            + word_heap::vec_growth::<Value>(key.len()) + key.iter().map(term).sum::<u64>()
            + word_heap::vec_growth::<(String, Value)>(equalities.len()) + equalities.iter().map(|(name,v)| word_heap::text(name)+term(v)).sum::<u64>()
        }
        _ => 0,
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#14 — PAGE admits discovery and target-lane metadata before allocation.
/// Temporary `(table, index)` images built while retaining a rule's complete
/// dependency envelope. Count them without allocating so PAGE lanes can admit
/// the buffer before `rules::probe_sites` clones any names into it.
fn rule_probe_sites_heap(rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> u64 {
    use crate::schema::{RuleExpr::*, RuleTerm};
    fn term(term: &RuleTerm) -> (usize, u64) {
        term.references().fold((0usize, 0u64), |(count, bytes), (table, _)| {
            (count.saturating_add(1), bytes.saturating_add(word_heap::text(table) + word_heap::text("byId")),
                )
        })
    }
    fn walk(rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> (usize, u64) {
        let add = |(ac, ab): (usize, u64), (bc, bb): (usize, u64)| {
            (ac.saturating_add(bc), ab.saturating_add(bb))
        };
        match rule {
            Eq(a, b) | Ne(a, b) | Compare(_, a, b) => add(term(a), term(b)),
            Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } | Created { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, term)| term)).fold(
                    (1, word_heap::text(table) + word_heap::text(index)),
                    |sum, item| add(sum, term(item)),
                ),
            And(rules) | Or(rules) => rules.iter().fold((0, 0), |sum, rule| add(sum, walk(rule))),
            Not(rule) => walk(rule),
            Allow | Deny | Public { .. } => (0, 0),
        }
    }
    let (count, names) = walk(rule);
    word_heap::vec_growth::<(String, String)>(count).saturating_add(names)
}

fn metered_dependency_table(
    meter: &mut crate::meter::Meter,
    read_tables: &mut BTreeSet<String>,
    table: &str,
) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if !read_tables.contains(table) {
        meter.heap(word_heap::text(table)
            + word_heap::insertion::<String, ()>(read_tables.len()))?;
        read_tables.insert(table.to_owned());
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — search state belongs to the generated word merge's direct lanes, never to an expression nested inside one.
#[derive(Clone)]
struct WordRead {
    admitted: bool,
    metered: bool,
    retained: bool,
    expiry: super::expiry::Pending,
    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    selected: Option<Vec<String>>,
    fields: Vec<String>,
    terms: Vec<String>,
}

// Compiled scans are shared images; a lane owns only its evaluated position.
type PreparedLane<'a> = (&'a Scan, Vec<Value>, Bounds, VecDeque<Value>, bool, Option<std::rc::Rc<WordRead>>,
);

struct MergeHead {
    key: Vec<Value>,
    row: Value,
    lane: usize,
    order: Order,
}

impl Ord for MergeHead {
    fn cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Ordering {
        let order = self.key.iter().zip(&other.key).find_map(|(a, b)| {
            let c = a.total_cmp(b);
            (c != Ordering::Equal).then_some(c)
        }).unwrap_or_else(|| self.key.len().cmp(&other.key.len()));
        let order = match self.order { Order::Asc => order.reverse(), Order::Desc => order };
        order.then_with(|| other.lane.cmp(&self.lane))
    }
}
impl PartialOrd for MergeHead { fn partial_cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Option<Ordering> { Some(self.cmp(other)) } }
impl PartialEq for MergeHead { fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool { self.cmp(other) == Ordering::Equal } }
impl Eq for MergeHead {}

// One cached probe answer avoids refetching an unchanged fact in a loop.
// Writes invalidate it; cache hits still pay the declared logical probe charge.
#[derive(PartialEq)]
pub(super) struct WitnessKey {
    revision: u64, table: String, index: String, key: Vec<Value>,
    equalities: Vec<(String, Value)>, current: bool,
}

pub(super) struct RuleProbe<'a, 'b> {
    pub(super) runner: &'a mut Runner<'b>,
    pub(super) allowance: &'a Authorization,
}
impl Probe for RuleProbe<'_, '_> {
    fn reference(&mut self, table: &str, id: &Value, field: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> { self.runner.rule_reference(table, id, field) }
    fn take_changes(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<(i64, super::rules::Verdict)>> { self.runner.witness_changes.take() }
    fn replace_expiry(&mut self, expiry: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> { self.runner.next_expiry.replace(expiry) }
    fn exists(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], equalities: &[(String, Value)]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.runner.rule_probe_exists(self.allowance, table, index, key, equalities)
    }
    fn created(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool {
        self.runner.created(table, index, key)
    }
}

/// The scanned prefix as the row a rule is judged against: each pinned column with its value.
fn prefix_row(index: &Index, prefix: &[Value]) -> Value {
    Value::Row(index.components.iter().zip(prefix).filter_map(|(part, value)| part.column().map(|name| (name.clone(), value.clone()))).collect::<std::collections::BTreeMap<_, _>>().into())
}

impl<'r> Runner<'r> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — the dependency envelope is schema-derived too; short-circuit probes cannot disclose a hidden answer through tables.
    pub(super) fn rule_dependencies(&mut self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) {
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        for (table, _) in sites {
            if let Some(key) = self.schema.maintain_for(&table).and_then(|m|m.row_key.as_ref()) {
                self.read_tables.insert(key.table.clone());
            }
            self.aggregate_source_dependency(&table);
            self.read_tables.insert(table);
        }
    }

    fn metered_aggregate_source_dependency(&mut self, table: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            .filter(|maintain| self.schema.tables[&maintain.source].is_expiring()) {
            metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &maintain.source)?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn metered_rule_dependencies(&mut self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let site_heap = rule_probe_sites_heap(rule);
        let site_mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(site_heap)?;
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        for (table, _) in sites {
            if let Some(row_key) = self.schema.maintain_for(&table)
                .and_then(|maintain| maintain.row_key.as_ref()) {
                metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &row_key.table)?;
            }
            self.metered_aggregate_source_dependency(&table)?;
            metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &table)?;
        }
        self.meter.release_row_heap(site_mark, site_heap);
        Ok(())
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.7 — trusted delivery avoids physical probes,
    // not the logical prefix verdict or its scratch admission.
    fn reserve_delivered_scan(&mut self, table: &Table, visibility: rules::Visibility, admitted: bool, query: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && (query || self.meter.cut == crate::meter::Cut::Abort) && !self.context.is_system()
            // An admitted merge lane already paid its guard/discovery proof.
            // Reserve exactly the native lane price, including an empty range.
            && self.merge_grant.is_none()
            && admitted && visibility.uniform && !visibility.decided_true && !table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable() {
            let allowance = [(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))];
            if self.meter.cut == crate::meter::Cut::Abort { self.authorized_scan(&allowance, |_, _| Ok(()))?; }
            else { self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &allowance, |_, _| Ok(()))?; }
            return Ok(true);
        }
        Ok(false)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — source invalidation is schema-derived, including empty and denied groups.
    pub(super) fn aggregate_source_dependency(&mut self, table: &str) {
        if let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            && self.schema.tables[&maintain.source].is_expiring() {
            self.read_tables.insert(maintain.source.clone());
        }
    }

    fn prefix_verdict(&mut self, name: &str, table: &Table, index: &Index, prefix: &[Value], rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr, uniform: bool) -> Result<(bool, super::expiry::Pending), RuntimeError> {
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        if let Some(through) = &table.through && let Some(id) = index.columns().iter().zip(prefix).find_map(|(name,v)| (name == &through.reference).then_some(v)) {
            let verdict = self.parent_verdict(name,through,id)?;
            if verdict == super::through::ParentVerdict::Unknown { self.unknown_fact(name, crate::schema::through::CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], "read through parent"); }
            let admitted = verdict == super::through::ParentVerdict::Readable;
            return Ok((admitted, if admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
            ));
        }
        let probe_row = prefix_row(index, prefix);
        let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — one prepaid, restoring scope: a partial conjunction denial costs the same static allowance as a uniform denial.
        let verdict = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(rule), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))], |runner, allowance| {
            rules::evaluate(rule, &viewer, None, &probe_row, false, &mut PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
        });
        self.trace_authorization(&verdict, name, Effect::Read, "read rule");
        let verdict = verdict?;
        if !verdict { self.filtered_clause(name, "read rule"); }
        let admitted = verdict && !self.read_capped();
        Ok((admitted, if uniform && admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
        ))
    }

    /// A page guard, judged once for the caller (`rules::page_guard_decision`):
    /// a row-independent grant, or a disjunct whose row columns the prefix pins,
    /// evaluated with the prefix's values. It charges the read rule's static
    /// probe count on either verdict, and records no filtered clause: a false
    /// guard leaves the per-row walk.
    fn grant_verdict(&mut self, table: &Table, index: &Index, prefix: &[Value], rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> Result<(bool, super::expiry::Pending), RuntimeError> {
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let probe_row = prefix_row(index, prefix);
        let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
        let verdict = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(rule), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))], |runner, allowance| {
            rules::evaluate(rule, &viewer, None, &probe_row, false, &mut PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
        })?;
        let admitted = verdict && !self.read_capped();
        Ok((admitted, if admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
        ))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — coverage applies before a missing candidate or empty page can return zero/complete.
    pub(super) fn aggregate_read_coverage(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], site: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.aggregate_read_coverage_inner(table, index, key, site, false)
    }

    fn aggregate_read_coverage_inner(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], site: &str, metered: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if metered { self.metered_aggregate_source_dependency(table)?; }
        else { self.aggregate_source_dependency(table); }
        if !self.device { return Ok(()); }
        let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table) else { return Ok(()); };
        if self.defer_receipt_maintain(maintain) {
            let group = self.receipt_read_group(maintain, index, key)?;
            self.receipt_maintain_pending(maintain, group.as_deref())?;
        }
        if let Some(k) = &maintain.row_key {
            // A projected view uses its member's acquisition; absence beyond that
            // horizon is unknown even though the projection has no order columns.
            if self.schema.tables[&k.table].sync.as_ref().is_none_or(|s| s.horizon.is_some()) {
                if metered { metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &k.table)?; }
                else { self.read_tables.insert(k.table.clone()); }
                // A held point value is checked against the member by live_row_view.
                let index_def = &self.schema.tables[table].indexes[index];
                if !index_def.unique || key.len() != index_def.components.len() {
                    let whole = self.schema.tables[&k.table].sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|sync| {
                        let Some(horizon) = &sync.horizon else { return false; };
                        let group = match &sync.audience {
                            crate::schema::Audience::Public => Some(vec![]),
                            crate::schema::Audience::Column(field) => index_def.columns().iter()
                                .position(|f| f == field).and_then(|i| key.get(i)).map(|v| vec![v.clone()]),
                            _ => None,
                        };
                        group.is_some_and(|group| {
                                matches!(self.tx.acquired_boundary(&k.table, &horizon.by, &group),
                            Ok(Some(crate::rows::AcquiredBoundary::Whole)))
                            })
                    });
                    if !whole { self.unknown_fact(table, index, key, site); }
                }
            }
            return Ok(());
        }
        let source = &self.schema.tables[&maintain.source];
        if self.schema.maintain_subset_unknown(table) || (source.is_expiring() && source.sync.as_ref().is_none_or(|sync| sync.horizon.is_some())) {
            if metered { metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &maintain.source)?; }
            else { self.read_tables.insert(maintain.source.clone()); }
            self.unknown_fact(table, index, key, site);
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn horizon_coverage(&mut self, scan: &Scan, prefix: &[Value], retained: bool) {
        if retained { self.beyond_horizon = true; self.components.note(|frame| { frame.beyond = true; frame.retained = true; }); }
        else { self.unknown_fact(&scan.table, &scan.index, prefix, &scan.site); }
        // @ref LLP 3000.016#2.2 — unknown inbox coverage withholds the whole query, including unshaped pages.
        if self.schema.timelines.iter().any(|timeline| scan.table == timeline.table("Feed")) { self.query_unavailable = true; }
    }

    /// Whether a row is past its table's `expire` column at this run's
    /// clock. Expired is absent: to every reader, on every host, before
    /// any rule (the sweep makes it physical). The system sees no more
    /// than anyone: an expired row contributes to nothing.
    pub(super) fn expired(&self, table: &Table, row: &Value) -> bool {
        self.context.now.is_some_and(|now| table.expired(row, now))
    }

    /// Whether a stored row is outside its published half-open read lifetime.
    /// Physical write resolution and cleanup use `expired` instead. A due row
    /// remains absent while its private opening entry is still pending: the
    /// bounded publisher has not emitted this row's partition transition yet.
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.003#4-advancing-time-without-an-application-job — a due ledger entry is the publication barrier.
    fn inactive_with_pending(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: &Value, refuse_pending: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let Some(now) = self.context.now else { return Ok(false); };
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        if !table.live(row, now) { return Ok(true); }
        if table.not_before.is_none() { return Ok(false); }
        let id = row.field("id")?.as_str()?;
        if !self.tx.time_audience_pending(table_name, id)? { return Ok(false); }
        if refuse_pending {
            self.query_unavailable = true;
            self.pending_opening = Some((table_name.to_owned(), id.to_owned()));
            return Err(RuntimeError::TimeAudiencePending);
        }
        Ok(true)
    }

    pub(super) fn inactive(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.inactive_with_pending(table_name, row, true)
    }

    /// A physical write target may be cancelled or rescheduled while its
    /// publication is pending. It remains unreadable to the author's program.
    pub(super) fn inactive_write_target(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.inactive_with_pending(table_name, row, false)
    }

    /// Step over a fetched row that is expired at this run's clock but not yet
    /// swept. It is the store's internal work: it never reaches the public
    /// meter or the shared authorization walk, so a read meters the same on
    /// every store and on a device that never received the row. The work is
    /// bounded by its own guard: each stepped row reserves a fixed 256 bytes
    /// of internal scratch plus its key, so one execution steps over at most
    /// about a million such rows before it refuses E_STORE_MEMORY.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.6 — the public meter charges only rows live at the read's clock.
    /// Returns whether `row` was expired (and so stepped over).
    pub(super) fn step_inactive(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: &Value, key: &[u8]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if !self.inactive(table_name, row)? { return Ok(false); }
        let bytes = EXPIRED_STEP + key.len() as u64;
        self.tx.refund(1);
        self.expired_steps = self.expired_steps.saturating_add(bytes);
        self.meter.scratch = self.meter.scratch.saturating_add(bytes);
        if self.expired_steps <= EXPIRED_STEP_GUARD { return Ok(true); }
        Err(word_heap::store_memory())
    }

    /// An unopened rule witness is absent without spending the shared
    /// authorization walk. Expired witnesses retain their established
    /// privacy bound; callers check the remaining upper lifetime separately.
    pub(super) fn step_unopened(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: &Value, key: &[u8]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let Some(now) = self.context.now else { return Ok(false); };
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        if table.not_before.is_none() { return Ok(false); }
        // Upper expiry wins when the two lifetime edges do not overlap. A row
        // which can never be live retains the ordinary expired-witness walk;
        // its private opening instant must not change authorization capacity.
        if table.expired(row, now) { return Ok(false); }
        let hidden = if !table.opened(row, now) { true } else {
            let id = row.field("id")?.as_str()?;
            let pending = self.tx.time_audience_pending(table_name, id)?;
            if pending {
                self.query_unavailable = true;
                self.pending_opening = Some((table_name.to_owned(), id.to_owned()));
                return Err(RuntimeError::TimeAudiencePending);
            }
            false
        };
        if !hidden { return Ok(false); }
        let bytes = EXPIRED_STEP + key.len() as u64;
        self.tx.refund(1);
        self.expired_steps = self.expired_steps.saturating_add(bytes);
        self.meter.scratch = self.meter.scratch.saturating_add(bytes);
        if self.expired_steps <= EXPIRED_STEP_GUARD { return Ok(true); }
        Err(word_heap::store_memory())
    }

    pub(super) fn note_expiry(&mut self, table: &Table, row: &Value) {
        if let Some(column) = table.expiry_column()
            && let Ok(Value::Int(at)) = row.field(column)
            && self.context.now.is_some_and(|now| *at > now)
        {
            self.next_expiry.note(*at);
        }
    }

    /// Delivered rows are authoritative except when a temporary audience
    /// can end on the device's clock without a server commit. Retained
    /// history stays readable; until-revoked content rechecks that audience.
    pub(super) fn trust_synced_read(&self, table: &Table) -> bool {
        self.device && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|sync| {
            table.leave == crate::schema::LeavePolicy::DeliveredHistory || match &sync.audience {
                crate::schema::Audience::Target { table, .. } => self.schema.table(table).is_none_or(|target| !target.read_lifetime_sensitive()),
                _ => true,
            }
        })
    }

    /// Whether the viewer may `effect` the row. The system principal is the
    /// store's owner and passes every rule; validation and constraints
    /// still run for it.
    pub(crate) fn check_rule(&mut self, table_name: &str, effect: Effect, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if effect != Effect::Read && self.timeline_may_write(table_name, effect) { return Ok(true); }
        if self.context.is_system() {
            return Ok(true);
        }
        if effect == Effect::Read && self.is_provisional(table_name, next)? { return self.provisional_readable(table_name, next); }
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        // A device holds a synced table's rows because the server delivered
        // each under the read rule and withdraws what it revokes; what
        // `retain delivered-history` kept stays readable here (LLP 3000 §7).
        if effect == Effect::Read && self.trust_synced_read(table) {
            return Ok(true);
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — item 7(a): an unnamed update rule judges old and next.
        let mut rule = table.rules.for_effect(effect).clone();
        if effect == Effect::Update && old.is_some() && table.judges_old_image() { rule = rule.old_and_next(); }
        if effect == Effect::Update && let Some(old) = old {
            for (field, authority) in &table.written_by {
                if !authority.audience && old.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone())) {
                    rule.fold_untouched_field(field, &table.columns[field]);
                }
            }
        }
        if effect == Effect::Read { self.rule_dependencies(&rule); }
        let bytes = self.rule_scratch(&rule);
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        let mut refused_image = None;
        let result = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&rule))], |runner, allowance| {
            let mut next = next.clone();
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut next, table);
            let old = old.map(|old| {
                let mut old = old.clone();
                materialize_optional_fields(&mut old, table);
                old
            });
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            if effect == Effect::Update && old.is_some() && runner.context.refusal_trace.is_some() {
                let (allowed, image) = runner.evaluate_update_rule(allowance, table_name, &rule, table.judges_old_image(), old.as_ref(), &next)?;
                refused_image = (!allowed).then_some(if table.update_transition { "named transition (old/next)" } else if table.judges_old_image() { image } else { "identical old/next images" });
                return Ok(allowed);
            }
            runner.evaluate_sequence_rule(allowance, table_name, &rule, effect, old.as_ref(), &next)
        }).map_err(|error| error.authorization_context(table_name, effect));
        self.trace_rule_result(&result, table_name, effect);
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if let Some(image) = refused_image {
            self.refusing_phase(table_name, "update rule", &format!("table authorization layer: update rule, {image}; see: guide rule-recipes update-images"));
        }
        result
    }

    /// A row by id, materialized, without a rule check.
    pub(crate) fn fetch(&mut self, _allowance: &Authorization, table_name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        let found = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(table) {
            self.live_lookup(_allowance, table_name, "byId", &table.indexes["byId"], &[Value::Id(id.into())], #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] None)?
        } else { self.authorized_scratch(crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table), &[], |runner, authority| {
            runner.tx.get(authority, table_name, id).map(|found| {
                        found.map(|mut row| {
                materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                row
            })
                    })
        })? };
        if self.device && (table.sync.is_none() || (found.is_none() && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|s| s.horizon.is_some()))) { self.unknown_fact(table_name, "byId", &[Value::Id(id.to_owned())], "write target"); }
        match found {
            Some(row) if !self.inactive(table_name, &row)? => Ok(Some(row)),
            _ => Ok(None),
        }
    }

    /// A server write may address an unopened row without making that image
    /// readable. Fixed decode admission prevents its payload size from
    /// selecting a public meter result.
    pub(crate) fn fetch_write_target(
        &mut self,
        allowance: &Authorization,
        table_name: &str,
        id: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        if self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .is_none_or(|table| table.not_before.is_none())
        {
            return self.fetch(allowance, table_name, id);
        }
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        if self.device {
            self.unknown_fact(
                table_name,
                "byId",
                &[Value::Id(id.to_owned())],
                "scheduled write target",
            );
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let found = self.authorized_scratch(
            crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table),
            &[],
            |runner, _| {
                runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id).map(|found| {
                    found.map(|mut row| {
                        materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                        row
                    })
                })
            },
        )?;
        Ok(found.filter(|row| !self.expired(table, row)))
    }

    /// The scan's prefix values, evaluated. A word term is not a value a
    /// store can seek to; `scan_words` takes those scans apart first.
    pub(super) fn prefix_values(&mut self, scan: &Scan) -> Result<Vec<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        scan.prefix
            .iter()
            .map(|key| match key {
                IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(expr) => self.expr(expr),
                IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(_) => Err(RuntimeError::Schema("a matching term is read through its words(..) index, never as a prefix value".into())),
            })
            .collect()
    }

    /// A `matching` read: the query's first term selects every word of the
    /// index it is a prefix of (one seek each, bounded by the meter for a
    /// uniform scope, otherwise at most `MAX_WORD_LANES`),
    /// and those words are merged as ordered lanes into one page, newest
    /// first when the index says so; every further term must also be a
    /// prefix of some word of the row (a filter over the page, never a
    /// second index). An empty term list matches nothing: its page is empty,
    /// not everything.
    fn scan_words(&mut self, scan: &Scan) -> Result<Vec<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.1 — a uniform matching scan is always lazy: the merge position appends
        // the row id when the ordering suffix lacks it.
        if rules::scan_visibility(self.schema, scan).uniform { return self.merge_lazy(None, Some(scan)); }
        let Some((merge, word)) = self.word_lanes(scan)? else {
            if self.page_depth == 0 && scan.opaque_cursor {
                self.record_page(scan.site.clone(), PagePosition { variant: scan.variant, view_suffix: None, table: scan.table.clone(), index: scan.index.clone(), order: scan.order, prefix_len: scan.prefix.len(), prefix: None, merge: true, interrupted: false, inclusive: false, shape_positions: Vec::new(), shape_resume: super::shaping::Resume::Never, next: None }, std::iter::empty())?;
            }
            return Ok(Vec::new());
        };
        self.merge_with_words(&merge, Some(&word))
    }

    /// Admit one scope, then discover its words. Both a scoped page and a
    /// uniform matching merge use exactly this validation and charge order.
    fn prepare_words(&mut self, scan: &Scan, bounds_validated: bool) -> Result<Option<(Vec<Value>, String, WordRead)>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = self.schema.table(&scan.table).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {}", scan.table)))?;
        let index = table.index(&scan.index).ok_or_else(|| {
            RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing index {}.{}", scan.table, scan.index))
        })?;
        let Some((position, fields)) = index.word_component() else {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no words(..) component for a matching read", scan.table, scan.index)));
        };
        let visibility = rules::scan_visibility(self.schema, scan);
        if visibility.uniform {
            // Scope slots and field names before collecting; expr charges evaluated values.
            self.meter.heap(word_heap::slots::<Value>(position) + word_heap::strings(fields))?;
        }
        let fields = fields.to_vec();
        let mut scope = Vec::with_capacity(position);
        let mut query = Value::Null;
        for key in &scan.prefix {
            match key {
                IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(expr) => scope.push(self.expr(expr)?),
                IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(expr) => query = self.expr(expr)?,
            }
        }
        if scope.len() != position {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} takes {position} prefix values before its words, not {}", scan.table, scan.index, scope.len())));
        }
        let text = match query {
            Value::String(text) => text,
            Value::Null => String::new(),
            other => {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("matching takes text, not {}", other.kind())));
            }
        };
        let mut terms = if visibility.uniform {
            word_heap::tokenize_limit(&mut self.meter, [&*text], crate::words::MAX_TERMS)?
        } else { crate::words::terms(&text) };
        // Validate every scan input before denial or empty word discovery can
        // return. Generated lanes retain the same bounds and cursor scope.
        if !bounds_validated { self.word_bounds(scan, table, index, None, visibility.uniform, false)?; }
        self.take(&scan.take)?;
        if visibility.uniform {
            self.word_note_table(&scan.table)?;
            self.word_dependencies(&table.rules.read)?;
            self.meter.heap(word_heap::text(&scan.site))?;
        } else {
            self.read_tables.insert(scan.table.clone());
            self.rule_dependencies(&table.rules.read);
        }
        self.meter.at(&scan.site);
        let mut retained = false;
        if self.device {
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.2 — retention has no horizon requirement, including positive existence; prepay and share one scope verdict.
            // Retained-group key scratch and coverage's diagnostic JSON/names
            // are bounded before the lookup or unknown_fact formats/clones them.
            if visibility.uniform && (table.leave == crate::schema::LeavePolicy::DeliveredHistory || table.sync.is_none()
                || (!scan.existence && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|sync| sync.horizon.is_some()))) {
                self.device_work(|runner| {
                    runner.meter.heap(256 * (192 + DisclosedKeys(&scope).heap() + word_heap::text(&scan.table) + word_heap::text(&scan.index) + word_heap::text(&scan.site)))
                })?;
            }
            retained = self.retained_group(&scan.table, &scan.index, &scope)?;
            if table.sync.is_none() { self.unknown_fact(&scan.table, &scan.index, &scope, &scan.site); self.components.note(|frame| frame.speculative = true); }
            else if !scan.existence && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|sync| sync.horizon.is_some()) {
                self.horizon_coverage(scan, &scope, retained);
            }
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — one verdict per matching scope, before word discovery and shared by its generated lanes.
        let parent_pinned = table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| {
            index.columns().iter().take(scope.len()).any(|f| f == &t.reference)
        });
        let mut admitted = !parent_pinned && (self.trust_synced_read(table) || self.context.is_system() || visibility.decided_true || table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable());
        let mut prefix_expiry = super::expiry::Pending::default();
        self.private_scan_authority(&scan.table, visibility.uniform, admitted)?;
        self.reserve_delivered_scan(table, visibility, admitted, false)?;
        if !admitted && !visibility.uniform { self.non_uniform = true; self.components.note(|frame| frame.non_uniform = true); }
        if terms.is_empty() {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        if !admitted {
            // Uniform word scopes can borrow their rule; simplification/cloning
            // is needed only by the existing partial-conjunction traversal.
            let partial = if visibility.uniform { None } else { rules::prefix_decision(self.schema, scan) };
            if let Some(rule) = if visibility.uniform { Some(&table.rules.read) } else { partial.as_ref() } {
            if visibility.uniform { self.meter.heap(key_verdict_heap(index, &scope, &self.context.viewer, rule))?; }
            let aborting = self.meter.cut == crate::meter::Cut::Abort;
            if aborting && !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)? { return Ok(None); }
            let (allowed, pending) = self.prefix_verdict(&scan.table, table, index, &scope, rule, visibility.uniform)?;
            if !allowed && !(self.device && parent_pinned) {
                return Ok(None);
            }
            prefix_expiry = pending;
            admitted = allowed && visibility.uniform;
            }
        }
        let first = terms.remove(0);
        Ok(Some((scope, first, WordRead { #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] selected: None, admitted, metered: admitted && visibility.uniform, retained, expiry: prefix_expiry, fields, terms },
        )))
    }

    fn discover_words(&mut self, scan: &Scan) -> Result<Option<(Vec<String>, Vec<Value>, WordRead)>, RuntimeError> {
        const MAX_WORD_LANES: usize = 32;
        let Some((scope, first, word)) = self.prepare_words(scan, false)? else { return Ok(None);
        };
        let table = &self.schema.tables[&scan.table];
        let index = &table.indexes[&scan.index];
        let position = index.word_component().unwrap().0;
        let admitted = word.admitted;
        let metered_words = word.metered;
