        let operations=bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),name)).map(|p|format!("{} ({})",p.program.name,p.program.kind.word())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let mut paths=vec!["server point/list/filter/view/search/count/exists/live-refetch: ordinary ruled reads".into(),"mutation valued write images: authorized final reread; logical refusals separately unproved".into()];
        if t.is_expiring()||t.not_before.is_some(){paths.push(format!("live rows only: not before {:?}, expire {:?}; server clock gates delivery",t.not_before,t.expire));}
        if let Some(sync)=&t.sync {paths.push(format!("sync/device envelope: {}; device access uses delivered snapshot; leave {:?}",sync.line(),t.leave));}
        if t.columns.values().any(|k|k.contains_asset()){paths.push("asset read: owning-row read rule AND applicable owner-private column authority; upload placement: insert/update authority".into());}
        if let Some(through)=&t.through {paths.push(format!("read through {}.{} -> {} (capture/live reduction unproved)",name,through.reference,through.parent));}
        for m in &bound.schema.maintains {if m.source==*name||m.target==*name {paths.push(format!("derived {} -> {} (independent aggregate audience)",m.source,if m.target.starts_with("$column:"){ "maintained column" }else{&m.target}));}}
        if !t.on_delete.is_empty(){paths.push(format!("incoming referential actions bypass child rules: {:?}",t.on_delete));}
        let mut grants=Vec::new();let mut pending=Vec::new();let mut visited=BTreeSet::new();
        for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete] {for dep in kernel::dependencies(r){pending.push((dep,r.quarry()));}}
        while let Some((dep,predicate))=pending.pop(){if !visited.insert(dep.clone()){continue;}let Some(g)=bound.schema.table(&dep)else{continue};
            grants.push(Grant{table:dep.clone(),predicate,writers:writers(bound,&dep)});
            for r in [&g.rules.read,&g.rules.insert,&g.rules.update,&g.rules.delete]{for next in kernel::dependencies(r){pending.push((next,r.quarry()));}}
        }
        Exposure{table:name.clone(),columns,effects,operations,paths,grants}
    }).collect()
}
/// Role labels retain complete predicates, including relationships. Every
/// entry is conditional; a roster's existence is never assumed for all users.
pub(super) fn roles(exposures:&[Exposure])->BTreeMap<String,Vec<String>> {
    let mut out:BTreeMap<String,Vec<String>>=BTreeMap::new();
    for x in exposures {for (effect,predicate) in &x.effects {
        if predicate=="deny"{continue;}
        let label=if predicate=="allow"||predicate=="public"||predicate.starts_with("public "){"every signed-in user".into()}else{format!("caller satisfying {predicate}")};
        out.entry(label).or_default().push(format!("{} {effect}: {predicate}; operations: {}",x.table,x.operations.join(", ")));
    }}out
}
//! Author-owned intent starts with a reviewable draft, never inferred policy.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#103-adoption — syntactic coverage is distinct from a proved invariant.
use super::*;

pub(super) fn missing(bound:&Bound)->Vec<String>{
    bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name,t)| {
        ([&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|!matches!(r,RuleExpr::Deny)) || !t.written_by.is_empty())
            && !bound.invariants.iter().any(|i| &i.table==*name)
    }).map(|(name,_)|name.clone()).collect()
}

/// Suggested declarations mirror enforcement, not the product specification.
/// The caller must keep this outside the compiled .q source set.
pub fn draft(bound:&Bound)->String{
    let mut out=String::from("-- DRAFT: NOT ACCEPTED. Generated from access rules, not the app specification.\n-- Confirm or correct each policy against the spec, then copy chosen declarations\n-- into an authored snapback/*.q file. This .q.draft file is never compiled.\n-- Check current roles, grant revocation, count/existence privacy, deadlines and\n-- required evidence separately. A copied wrong rule cannot catch itself.\n-- snapback4 guide security\n\n");
    for (name,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){
        for (effect,rule) in [("read",&t.rules.read),("insert",&t.rules.insert),("update",&t.rules.update),("delete",&t.rules.delete)] {
            if matches!(rule,RuleExpr::Deny)||covered(bound,name,effect){continue;}
            let predicate=if matches!(rule,RuleExpr::Public{..}){"allow".into()}else{rule.quarry()};
            if predicate.contains('$') || effect=="update" && t.update_transition {
                out.push_str(&format!("-- {name}.{effect}: write intent explicitly; captured/transition rule: {predicate}\n"));
            }else {out.push_str(&format!("-- Candidate from {name} {effect} rule; AUTHOR MUST CONFIRM.\ninvariant {name}.{effect} only {predicate}\n"));}
        }
        for (column,authority) in &t.written_by {
            out.push_str(&format!("-- {name}.{column}: effective field-owner patch authority .{} = viewer bypasses the table update rule; declare column-write intent from the spec.\n",authority.owner));
        }
    }
    out
}
fn covered(bound:&Bound,table:&str,effect:&str)->bool{
    bound.invariants.iter().any(|i|i.table==table && i.operation.is_none() && i.column.is_none()
        && (i.target==effect || i.target=="write" && matches!(effect,"insert"|"update")))
}
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize)]
pub struct UncoveredExposure {pub table:String,pub effect:String,pub audience:String,pub row_predicate:String,pub column:Option<String>}
pub(super) fn uncovered(bound:&Bound,exposures:&[Exposure])->Vec<UncoveredExposure>{
    let mut out=exposures.iter().flat_map(|x|x.effects.iter().filter(|(effect,rule)|rule.as_str()!="deny"&&!covered(bound,&x.table,effect)).map(|(effect,predicate)|UncoveredExposure{
        table:x.table.clone(),effect:effect.clone(),audience:if predicate=="allow"||predicate.starts_with("public"){"every signed-in user".into()}else{format!("caller satisfying {predicate}")},row_predicate:predicate.clone(),column:None,
    })).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    for (name,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){for (column,authority) in &t.written_by{
        if covered(bound,name,"update")||bound.invariants.iter().any(|i|i.table==*name&&i.operation.is_none()&&i.column.as_ref()==Some(column)&&i.target=="write"){continue;}
        out.push(UncoveredExposure{table:name.clone(),effect:"update".into(),column:Some(column.clone()),audience:format!("owner .{} = viewer",authority.owner),row_predicate:format!("field-owner patch authority for {column}; table update rule may deny")});
    }}out
}

/// Exercise every authored query/mutation with cross-principal candidates.
/// This is conformance exploration, not a guessed spec oracle: only declared
/// policies and replayed violations can fail security acceptance.
pub fn operations(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
    histories::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
    assets::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
    let fixtures=match readback(fixture){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(e);return;}};
    let has_fixture=fixtures.values().any(|r|!r.is_empty());
    if fixtures.values().map(Vec::len).sum::<usize>()>1000{campaign.unsupported.insert("operation fixture matrix exceeds 1000 rows; provide a focused security fixture".into());return;}
    let mut viewers=histories::viewers(bound);
    for (table,rows) in &fixtures{for row in rows{for (c,k) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns{if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)&&let Ok(Value::Id(p))=row.field(c)&&!viewers.contains(p){viewers.push(p.clone());}}}}
    for p in &bound.programs{
        if !matches!(p.program.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)||p.program.native{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: trusted/system/opaque operation",p.program.name));continue;}
        let target=bound.schema.visible_tables().find(|(t,_)|super::audit::mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),t)).or_else(||bound.schema.visible_tables().next()).map(|(t,_)|t.clone());
        let Some(target)=target else{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: no table population for generated arguments",p.program.name));continue;};
        let dummy=Invariant{table:target,target:"read".into(),column:None,predicate:"allow".into(),site:crate::Site::default(),policy:Some(RuleExpr::Allow),operation:Some(p.program.name.clone()),requirements:Vec::new(),syntax:None,unsupported:None};
        let cases=histories::argument_cases(&p.program.args,campaign);
        for (variant,mut args) in cases.into_iter().enumerate(){
            let rows=if has_fixture{fixtures.clone()}else{match snapshot(bound,&dummy,variant){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.program.name));break;}}};
            for (arg,kind) in &p.program.args {if let ir::ArgType::Ref{table}=kind&&let Some(row)=rows.get(table).and_then(|rs|rs.first())&&let Ok(id)=row.field("id"){args.insert(arg.clone(),id.clone());}}
            for viewer in &viewers{
                let mut engine=match if has_fixture {fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())}else{engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.matrix",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]))}{Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.program.name));continue;}};
                campaign.attempted+=1;*campaign.operations.entry(p.program.name.clone()).or_default()+=1;
                if p.program.kind==ProgramKind::Query{
                    match engine.query(&p.program.name,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){Ok(_)=>{},Err(e) if e.code=="E_INPUT"=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: generated arguments rejected as E_INPUT",p.program.name));},Err(_)=>campaign.refused+=1,}
                }else{
                    match engine.mutate(&p.program.name,None,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){Ok(result) if matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Failed{..})=>campaign.refused+=1,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: matrix engine error {e}",p.program.name));},_=>{},}
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

fn fork(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,probes:Vec<Program>)->Result<Engine,String>{
    let store=fixture.store().fork_memory().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    let mut programs=bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>();programs.extend(probes);
    Engine::new_at(store,bound.schema.clone(),programs,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())
}
# Rules

**Start here:** Reactions inherit their message's conversation membership; this excerpt uses the complete schema below. The dotted rule and audience are sugar for `read through` and inferred `sync`, including parent lifetime and insert authority.

```quarry
table reactions:
  message: messages
  immutable message
  read <- exists members[.message.conversation, viewer]
  sync to members[.message.conversation] last 500 by .id
```

- `E_READ_THROUGH`: the path must spell the whole parent rule; use a required immutable reference without a default, and immutable parent rule/expiry fields.
- `E_QUARRY_SELF_EQUALITY`: a nested predicate compares its row to itself; capture the outer value in a local before entering that predicate.
- `E_RULE`: a write lacks authority (mutations write as the caller; omitted rules deny); require a live readable parent and the child's insert rule, and under `dev` read the refusing clause with `snapback4 why recent`.

## Reference

### Hops, operators and read through
Write rules and `after insert` keys allow three live hops: `.reaction.message.conversation`, including `exists members[.message.conversation, viewer]`. `messages[id = .message].conversation` is equivalent. Every target must be live and readable; absent, expired or hidden targets remain unknown under `not` and `= null`. Program lookups (`t[id] !`, `exists`) are reads too: a row the caller cannot read is NOT_FOUND there, before any later `require` or the delete/update rule runs, so give a moderator read access to what they moderate, or delete by id without a lookup and let the delete rule decide (`guide refusals` has the recipe). `check` notes N_UNREADABLE_PROBE on guards without proved read authority; use unique/cap/after insert constraints for global absence across hidden rows.
Each occurrence prepays one probe, including skipped branches. `check --cost`/`why` list them; prediction needs every read covered.
Insert uses the new image; an unnamed `update <- R` must hold for both the row before and the row after the write (pair `update <- .owner = viewer` with `immutable owner`); a named `update (old, next)` judges exactly what it names; delete uses its pre-delete image; final obligations resolve at transaction end. Read-through capture/lifetimes stay unchanged. Null guards narrow only the same term and image.
Beyond three hops, private/maintained fields and ephemeral targets refuse E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP with concrete mutation `require` checks. A rule says `=`/`!=` over `.col`, `viewer` and literals, `exists`/`created` probes and `and`/`or`/`not`; it orders (`<`, `>=`) only an insert row's own required int/time/decimal/money columns (`.stars >= 1`; an integer literal beside money is minor units) and `update (old, next)` images of the same types. The clock, arithmetic, helper calls and `else CODE` are E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR: a `require … else CODE` in the mutation (`snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR` prints the table of rule forms).

```quarry
table orgs:
  read <- exists members[.id, viewer]
  insert <- allow
  online only
table members:
  org: orgs
  principal: principal
  unique org, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer
  insert <- created orgs[.org] or exists members[.org, viewer]
  online only
table issues:
  org: orgs
  title: text <= 200
  comments: count comments by issue
  immutable org
  read through .org
  insert <- allow
  online only
table comments:
  issue: issues
  author: principal
  body: text <= 2000
  immutable issue
  read through .issue
  insert <- .author = viewer
  delete <- .author = viewer
  online only
query discussion(issue: issues, c: cursor ?):
  comments where .issue = issue first 20 by .id after c
```

### A child inherits its parent's readers
A child names one required immutable reference, without a default, to an ordinary application table. `read through` is its whole read rule; put service grants on the parent. The parent's read-rule fields and authored expiry must be immutable. An insert also needs a live parent readable by the writer, before the child's insert rule runs. The engine captures those inputs; they travel with sync images but never appear in query rows or accept payload values.

Each child expires no later than its parent, through any number of levels. A parent's delete needs read authority and waits until its children are deleted and expired children swept. Its id cannot be reused while physical children remain. A mutable child deadline can shorten its life but cannot extend it past the parent.

`sync` infers exactly the parent's substituted audience after service grants are removed. A conjunction such as membership and an archived flag stays `online only`; `check --verbose` explains its server reads. A parent-pinned page resolves authorization once, while completeness still requires the child's own coverage. An unknown or mismatched parent takes the server fallback. Retained history stays marked `retained: true` beside the server's current page. On these syncing delivered-history children, `retained` is row metadata; name an application column `wasRetained` instead. Row shapes and spreads preserve the mark.

A device predicts a plain child insert only with a live parent covered by its current partition, or one predicted in the same pending chain. An unheld, expired or retained-only parent queues without a row. Upserts and `insert … if absent` need complete child-group coverage to prove absence; changed unique keys or cap groups need their populations covered. A parent delete requires full current child coverage and no live children. Expiry uses the later of the device's supplied clock and the last atomically applied server time.

### A child follows a mutable parent audience

`read through live .parent` maintains the whole current parent rule. Require `immutable parent` without a default and `cap N by parent`. Parent writes prepay maximum fanout, update children atomically and queue on devices. Bare `read through` stays captured.

Use ordinary nonexpiring endpoints/probes and bounded plain indexes. Chains, derived/private/receipt endpoints and writing reference actions refuse. Sync requires one exact all-current/until-revoked audience; other whole rules work online.

`N_LIVE_PARENT_COST` means fixed fanout exceeds ordinary ceilings. Reduce caps or row/index bounds, or maintain manually. Parent writes queue on devices.

Parent/roster changes invalidate; matching copies and child coverage prove authority. Adoption validates copies; restoration uses current parents. Rebuild for `live-parent-copy-v1`. Bulk calls share one job account. See `guide rule-recipes current-roster`.

### Clique receipts and reactions
Clique receipts and reactions need only their message reference. The unread view uses the captured conversation field and inherits its rule, without an explicit `read` or copied deadline. These parents stay online, so reaction pages use the server:

```quarry
table conversations:
  read <- exists members[.id, viewer]
  insert <- allow
  online only

table members:
  conversation: conversations
  principal: principal
  immutable conversation, principal
  unique conversation, principal
  read <- exists members[.conversation, viewer]
  insert <- created conversations[.conversation] or exists members[.conversation, viewer]
  online only

table messages:
  conversation: conversations
  sender: principal
  body: text <= 2000
  until: time ?
  immutable conversation, until
  read <- exists members[.conversation, viewer]
  insert <- .sender = viewer and exists members[.conversation, viewer]
  expire at .until
  online only

table receipts:
  message: messages
  principal: principal
  state: int
  immutable message, principal
  unique message, principal
  read through .message
  insert <- created messages[.message] or .principal = viewer
  update <- .principal = viewer
  online only

table reactions:
  message: messages
  immutable message
  read <- exists members[.message.conversation, viewer]
  sync to members[.message.conversation] last 500 by .id
  principal: principal
  emoji: text <= 20
  immutable principal
  unique message, principal
  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists members[.message.conversation, viewer]
  delete <- .principal = viewer and exists members[.message.conversation, viewer]

view unread: count receipts where .state < 2 by .message.conversation, .principal
  online only

query unreadFor(conversation: conversations):
  unread[conversation, viewer]
query received(message: messages, c: cursor ?):
  receipts where .message = message first 50 by .principal after c { id, principal, state }
query reacted(message: messages, c: cursor ?):
  reactions where .message = message first 50 by .principal after c { id, principal, emoji }
```

### Row rules, probes and rule spelling

A bare `exists` probe in a table rule never inherits the target's read rule: it tests the keyed row even when the caller cannot read it. Program `exists` is a caller-filtered read. Dotted membership hops in read rules are only `read through` (whole parent rule, immutable captures and inherited lifetime). An online-only one-hop equality may instead lower to its explicit keyed probe, as described below; it also inherits no target read rule. For the parent's current department roster, use `guide rule-recipes current-roster`.

A view grouped directly by the parent reference is read by key, such as `totals[issue]`; scanning its groups is refused. An unknown parent or unknown child coverage leaves that device count incomplete, never an invented zero. For independent row rules, use the comparisons and keyed probes below.

```quarry
table docs:
  ownerId: principal
  title: text
  state: enum('draft', 'final')
  read <- .ownerId = viewer or exists editors[.id, viewer]                  -- allow | deny | public ['why'] | .col = viewer | .col != 'x' | exists t[..] | and | or | not
  insert <- .ownerId = viewer and .state = 'draft'
  update (old, next) <- old.ownerId = viewer and next.ownerId = viewer and old.state != 'final'   -- both images of an update
  delete <- .ownerId = viewer

//! Compiled examples first; diagnostic details are under `why`.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Shapes

Shapes select returned fields and reuse result layouts.

```quarry
query nestedRoster(c: cursor ?):
  return members where .principal = viewer first 20 by .roomId after c { roomId, roster: members[roomId = .roomId], room: rooms[.roomId] { name } }
```

The outer page selects your memberships; its named inner key binds each room's roster.
`room` expands to an object, or null if missing/hidden. Each `where` binds its own `.` row.

## Limits

- Comparing a filtered row column with itself is refused, except optional-column `<=` and `>=` presence tests: E_QUARRY_SELF_EQUALITY.
- Spread a present row or object with known fields: E_QUARRY_SPREAD.
- Write each explicit field once: E_QUARRY_DUPLICATE_FIELD.
- Values joined by branches must have compatible row kinds: E_QUARRY_SHAPE_JOIN.
- Move or narrow a shape that cannot fit the meter: E_SHAPE_EXCEEDS_METER.

## Details

Shapes choose the fields returned from a row or page.
Name a shape to reuse the same result layout across reads.

```quarry
shape brief = { id, body, author: .authorId }                        -- a named shape: bare columns, and `.` for the row

query shapeForms(roomId: rooms, messageId: messages, c: cursor ?):
  page = messages where .roomId = roomId after c last 20 by .at { *, room: rooms[.roomId], count: messageCounts[.roomId] }   -- over a page: `*` spreads every column; a point read inside is null for a hidden row
  row = rooms[roomId] { id, name, tagCount: count(.tags) }           -- over a row: an object; over a nullable row: null when the row is
  named = messages[messageId] brief                                  -- a named shape by its name
  nested = rooms[roomId] { ...card, extra: 1 }                        -- `...name` spreads a named shape's fields
  return { page, row, named, nested }
```

```quarry
table listings:
  title: text
  city: text
  public
table bookings:
  guest: principal
  listing: listings
  start: time
  read <- .guest = viewer
query myBookings(c: cursor ?):
  return bookings where .guest = viewer last 20 by .start after c { *, listing: listings[.listing] { title, city } }
query listingChoice(key: listings, flag: bool):
  p = listings[key] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return ({ ...p } if flag else { ...(p { * }) }) { title: 'override', * }
```

Here `listing` is the expanded object, or null for a missing or hidden listing.

In the first example, table and shape names are the language card's. A shape over a page is
a bounded loop; its fields are the row's columns by bare name, `*` for all
of them, `...shape` for a named shape's fields, and `name: expr` with `.`
as the row. There is no `select` spelling.
A table-typed helper accepts that table's row or reference. A reference's
field reads its row: `.slot.day` is `slots[.slot].day`, null when missing or
hidden; `slots[.slot] ! else NOT_FOUND` refuses instead. Shapes are projections,
not declared helper-argument types.
A row-based shape keeps `pending?: boolean` from its base, including `*`, named
spreads and subsequent shapes. It is this device's unconfirmed change, absent
after confirmation; it is not a schema column or metadata on a containing record.

## Shapes and helpers

`guide reads` owns paging and continuation; `guide programs` owns helpers.
"##;
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        self.0.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::Release);
    }
}
fn limited(request: Request, shared: &Shared) {
    let mut refusal = Refusal::new(
        "E_RATE_LIMIT",
        "auth",
        "too many identity requests; retry later",
    )
    .guide("personas");
    refusal.retryable = true;
    respond_timed(request, shared, 429, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal) }));
}

//! Delivery metadata has a supplemental ledger, never the answer's meter.
// @ref LLP 3000.019#4 — the SDK reports only images delivered by the platform.
// @ref LLP 3000.022#5 — reserve stamping, origins, traversal and wire bytes independently.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{
    meter::Ceilings,
    schema::DeliveryBinding,
    value::{ObjectFields, SourceIdentity, Value},
};
use serde::{Serialize, Serializer};
use std::{
    collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet},
    sync::{
        Arc, OnceLock,
        atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering},
    },
};

// @ref LLP 3000.022#5 — unrelated reads pay no supplemental work. Membership
// belongs to the installed schema; its lazily built index is shared by readers.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
pub struct Lookup(Arc<LookupState>);
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
struct LookupState {
    tables: BTreeSet<String>,
    index: OnceLock<Arc<BTreeMap<String, usize>>>,
}
// Runtime caches never change schema equality, its hash or its wire form.
impl PartialEq for Lookup {
    fn eq(&self, _: &Self) -> bool { true }
}
impl Eq for Lookup {}
impl crate::schema::Schema {
    pub(crate) fn prepare_delivery_lookup(&mut self) {
        self.delivery_lookup = Lookup(Arc::new(LookupState {
            tables: self.delivery.iter().map(|binding| binding.table.clone()).collect(),
            index: OnceLock::new(),
        }));
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub struct DeliveryMeter {
    pub steps: u64,
    pub heap_bytes: u64,
    pub output_bytes: u64,
}
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
struct Heap {
    live: AtomicU64,
    peak: AtomicU64,
}
#[derive(Debug)]
pub(crate) struct Lease {
    heap: Arc<Heap>,
    bytes: u64,
}
impl Drop for Lease {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        self.heap.live.fetch_sub(self.bytes, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }
}
#[derive(Debug)]
pub(crate) struct Ledger {
    limits: DeliveryMeter,
    used: DeliveryMeter,
    heap: Option<Arc<Heap>>,
    pub partial: bool,
    stamping_stopped: bool,
}
impl Ledger {
    fn new(ceilings: Ceilings) -> Self {
        Self {
            limits: DeliveryMeter {
                steps: ceilings.steps / 4,
                heap_bytes: ceilings.heap_bytes / 4,
                output_bytes: (ceilings.output_bytes / 4).max(128),
            },
            used: DeliveryMeter::default(),
            heap: None,
            partial: false,
            stamping_stopped: false,
        }
    }
    fn reserve(&mut self, steps: u64, heap: u64, output: u64) -> bool {
        let live = self
            .heap
            .as_ref()
            .map_or(0, |heap| heap.live.load(Ordering::Relaxed));
        let heap = heap.saturating_add(if heap != 0 && self.heap.is_none() {
            64
        } else {
            0
        });
        if steps > self.limits.steps.saturating_sub(self.used.steps)
            || heap > self.limits.heap_bytes.saturating_sub(live)
            || output
                > self
                    .limits
                    .output_bytes
                    .saturating_sub(self.used.output_bytes)
        {
            self.partial = true;
            self.stamping_stopped = true;
            return false;
        }
        self.used.steps += steps;
        self.used.output_bytes += output;
        if heap != 0 {
            let state = self.heap.get_or_insert_with(Arc::default);
            state.live.fetch_add(heap, Ordering::Relaxed);
            state.peak.fetch_max(live + heap, Ordering::Relaxed);
        }
        true
    }
    fn tracking(&mut self, steps: u64, heap: u64) -> bool {
        !self.stamping_stopped && self.reserve(steps, heap, 0)
    }
    fn lease(&self, bytes: u64) -> Lease {
        Lease {
            heap: self.heap.as_ref().expect("reserved metadata heap").clone(),
            bytes,
        }
    }
    fn meter(&self) -> DeliveryMeter {
        DeliveryMeter {
            heap_bytes: self
                .heap
                .as_ref()
                .map_or(0, |heap| heap.peak.load(Ordering::Relaxed)),
            ..self.used
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Debug)]
pub(crate) struct Coordinates {
    pub table: String,
    pub id: String,
    pub group: Arc<str>,
    pub position: i64,
    pub position_column: String,
    _heap: Lease,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub(crate) struct Origin {
    pub source: Arc<Coordinates>,
    pub column: Arc<str>,
    _heap: Arc<Lease>,
}
#[derive(Debug)]
pub(crate) struct Image {
    pub coordinates: Option<Arc<Coordinates>>,
    pub fields: BTreeMap<String, Origin>,
    _heap: Lease,
}
#[derive(Default)]
pub(super) struct Origins {
    fields: BTreeMap<String, Origin>,
    heap: Option<Lease>,
}
#[derive(Default)]
pub(super) struct Tracking {
    ledger: Option<Ledger>,
    pub last: Option<Origin>,
    index: Option<Arc<BTreeMap<String, usize>>>,
    variables: BTreeMap<String, (Origin, Lease)>,
    // Removing an entry need not free its BTree node. Retain node charges
    // until the whole map is dropped; individual leases own only the names.
    variable_nodes: Option<Lease>,
}

impl Tracking {
    pub(super) fn field(&mut self, fields: &ObjectFields, name: &str) {
        self.last = None;
        if fields.delivery_image().is_none() {
            return;
        }
        if let Some(ledger) = &mut self.ledger
            && ledger.tracking(1, 0)
        {
            self.last = fields.field_origin(name);
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct Envelope {
    pub table: String,
    pub mutation: String,
    pub args: Vec<String>,
    pub fingerprint: String,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub compact: Option<String>,
    pub rows: Vec<(String, i64)>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")]
    pub partial: bool,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Manifest {
    Partial,
    Bindings(Vec<Envelope>),
}
impl Serialize for Manifest {
    fn serialize<S: Serializer>(&self, s: S) -> Result<S::Ok, S::Error> {
        match self {
            Self::Partial => s.serialize_str("partial"),
            Self::Bindings(bindings) => bindings.serialize(s),
        }
//! Conservative permission map; conditional predicates are never universal grants.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#5-exposure-report — include grant writers and implicit effects.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::ir;
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize)]
pub struct Grant {pub table:String,pub predicate:String,pub writers:Vec<String>}
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize)]
pub struct Exposure {pub table:String,pub columns:BTreeMap<String,String>,pub effects:BTreeMap<String,String>,pub operations:Vec<String>,pub paths:Vec<String>,pub grants:Vec<Grant>}
pub(super) fn mentions(json:&serde_json::Value,table:&str)->bool {
    match json {serde_json::Value::Object(fields)=>fields.iter().any(|(k,v)| k=="table"&&v.as_str()==Some(table)||mentions(v,table)),serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|x|mentions(x,table)),_=>false}
}
pub(super) fn authored_refusal(json:&serde_json::Value)->bool {
    match json {serde_json::Value::Object(fields)=>fields.get("Require").is_some()||fields.values().any(authored_refusal),serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(authored_refusal),_=>false}
}
fn writers(bound:&Bound,name:&str)->Vec<String> {
    let mut out=Vec::new();let t=&bound.schema.tables[name];
    for effect in [Effect::Insert,Effect::Update] {out.push(format!("{effect} rule: {}",t.rules.for_effect(effect).quarry()));}
    for p in &bound.programs {for (table,kind) in ir::write_tables(&p.program) {if table==name {out.push(format!("{} ({kind}, {}; {})",p.program.name,p.program.kind.word(),if p.program.kind==ir::ProgramKind::Job {"system boundary"}else{"caller rules/field authority"}));}}}
    for (column,authority) in &t.written_by {out.push(format!("column {column}: owner .{} can patch under field authority",authority.owner));}
    for m in &bound.schema.maintains {if m.target==name||m.private.as_ref().is_some_and(|p|p.table==name){out.push(format!("system maintain {} from {}",m.target,m.source));}}
    if !t.on_delete.is_empty(){out.push(format!("referential actions: {:?}",t.on_delete));}
    if bound.programs.iter().any(|p|p.program.native)||!bound.schema.action_specs.is_empty()||!bound.schema.effects.is_empty(){out.push("native/worker/external paths: opaque trusted writers (not an absence claim)".into());}
    out.push("operator/system seed, import, restore and activation (trusted boundary)".into());out.sort();out.dedup();out
}
pub(super) fn exposures(bound:&Bound)->Vec<Exposure> {
    bound.schema.visible_tables().map(|(name,t)| {
        let columns=t.columns.iter().filter(|(c,_)|!t.engine_field(c)).map(|(c,k)| {
            let authority=t.written_by.get(c).map(|a|format!("; written by .{}; {}",a.owner,if a.audience{"audience readable"}else{"private to owner"})).unwrap_or_default();
            (c.clone(),format!("{k}{authority}"))
        }).collect();
        let effects=[("read",t.rules.read.quarry()),("insert",t.rules.insert.quarry()),("update",t.update_rule_judged().quarry()),("delete",t.rules.delete.quarry())].into_iter().map(|(k,v)|(k.into(),v)).collect();
        let operations=bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),name)).map(|p|format!("{} ({})",p.program.name,p.program.kind.word())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let mut paths=vec!["server point/list/filter/view/search/count/exists/live-refetch: ordinary ruled reads".into(),"mutation valued write images: authorized final reread; logical refusals separately unproved".into()];
        if t.is_expiring()||t.not_before.is_some(){paths.push(format!("live rows only: not before {:?}, expire {:?}; server clock gates delivery",t.not_before,t.expire));}
        if let Some(sync)=&t.sync {paths.push(format!("sync/device envelope: {}; device access uses delivered snapshot; leave {:?}",sync.line(),t.leave));}
        if t.columns.values().any(|k|k.contains_asset()){paths.push("asset read: owning-row read rule AND applicable owner-private column authority; upload placement: insert/update authority".into());}
        if let Some(through)=&t.through {paths.push(format!("read through {}.{} -> {} (capture/live reduction unproved)",name,through.reference,through.parent));}
        for m in &bound.schema.maintains {if m.source==*name||m.target==*name {paths.push(format!("derived {} -> {} (independent aggregate audience)",m.source,if m.target.starts_with("$column:"){ "maintained column" }else{&m.target}));}}
        if !t.on_delete.is_empty(){paths.push(format!("incoming referential actions bypass child rules: {:?}",t.on_delete));}
        let mut grants=Vec::new();let mut pending=Vec::new();let mut visited=BTreeSet::new();
        for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete] {for dep in kernel::dependencies(r){pending.push((dep,r.quarry()));}}
        while let Some((dep,predicate))=pending.pop(){if !visited.insert(dep.clone()){continue;}let Some(g)=bound.schema.table(&dep)else{continue};
            grants.push(Grant{table:dep.clone(),predicate,writers:writers(bound,&dep)});
            for r in [&g.rules.read,&g.rules.insert,&g.rules.update,&g.rules.delete]{for next in kernel::dependencies(r){pending.push((next,r.quarry()));}}
        }
        Exposure{table:name.clone(),columns,effects,operations,paths,grants}
    }).collect()
}
/// Role labels retain complete predicates, including relationships. Every
/// entry is conditional; a roster's existence is never assumed for all users.
pub(super) fn roles(exposures:&[Exposure])->BTreeMap<String,Vec<String>> {
    let mut out:BTreeMap<String,Vec<String>>=BTreeMap::new();
    for x in exposures {for (effect,predicate) in &x.effects {
        if predicate=="deny"{continue;}
        let label=if predicate=="allow"||predicate=="public"||predicate.starts_with("public "){"every signed-in user".into()}else{format!("caller satisfying {predicate}")};
        out.entry(label).or_default().push(format!("{} {effect}: {predicate}; operations: {}",x.table,x.operations.join(", ")));
    }}out
}
//! A row-read verdict and field projection together authorize result pricing.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, ir::Read, meter::Meter,
    schema::{Effect, Index}, value::{Disclosed, Value}};

// Only successful row authorization in this module can mint this evidence.
pub(crate) struct RowAuthorization(());
pub(crate) struct AuthorizedRow<'a> {
    projection: Disclosed<'a>,
    authority: RowAuthorization,
    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5 — a row's witness deadline is provisional until an answer emits it.
    expiry: super::expiry::Pending,
}
impl AuthorizedRow<'_> {
    pub(super) fn expiry(&self) -> super::expiry::Pending { self.expiry }
    pub(super) fn scalar_heap(&self, field: &str) -> u64 { self.projection.scalar_heap(field, &self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn heap_bytes(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.heap_bytes(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn projection_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.projection_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn materialization_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.materialization_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn into_value(self) -> Value { self.projection.into_value() }
    pub(super) fn into_parts(self) -> (Value, RowAuthorization) { (self.projection.into_value(), self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn charge(self, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        meter.heap(self.heap_bytes() + self.projection_heap() + self.materialization_heap())?;
        Ok(self.into_value())
    }
}

impl RowAuthorization {
    pub(super) fn project(self, projection: Disclosed<'_>) -> AuthorizedRow<'_> { AuthorizedRow { projection, authority:self, expiry: Default::default() } }
}

impl<'r> Runner<'r> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — member reads share the row-read and field-disclosure capability.
    pub(super) fn authorized_member(&mut self, name: &str, id: &str, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] trusted: bool) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // @ref LLP 3000.024#decision — this id came from an authorized head in
        // this read-only transaction. Price its public image before decoding,
        // then recheck the rule with a separate witness reservation. Neither
        // hidden/expired images nor private fields can select actual-size admission.
        #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
        if trusted && !self.device && !table.read_lifetime_sensitive() && table.recorded.is_empty()
            && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a|a.audience) {
            if !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)? { return Ok(None); }
            self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::text(id) + crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<Value>(1))?;
            let key = [Value::Id(id.into())];
            let Some((Some(row),_)) = self.scan_authorized(name,crate::schema::BY_ID,&key,&Default::default(),crate::rows::Dir::Asc,1,true,true,None)?.pop() else { return Ok(None); };
            let materialization = row.materialization_heap();
            let prepay = self.meter.mark();
            self.meter.heap(materialization)?;
            let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
            let row = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes,super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read),
                )], |runner,_| {
                let row = runner.authorize_image(name,row.into_value())?;
                if let Some(row) = &row { runner.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); }
                Ok(row)
            });
            // The value now owns the default. Transfer its prepay to charge(),
            // keeping only one retained reservation after the recheck.
            self.meter.release_row_heap(prepay, materialization);
            let row = row?;
            if row.is_some() { expiry.emit(); }
            return row.map(|row|row.charge(&mut self.meter)).transpose();
        }
        let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table).saturating_add(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read));
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let row = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::Examined, 1), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read),
                )], |runner, scope| {
            let Some(mut row) = runner.tx.get(scope, name, id)? else { return Ok(None); };
            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
            let until = table.deadline(&row);
            let authorized = runner.authorize_image(name, row)?;
            if let Some(row) = &authorized { runner.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); runner.next_expiry.note_option(until); }
            Ok(authorized)
        })?;
        if row.is_some() { expiry.emit(); }
        row.map(|row|row.charge(&mut self.meter)).transpose()
    }

    pub(super) fn authorize_image(&mut self, name: &str, row: Value) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a denied or expired candidate's witness deadlines are discarded with its scope.
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        if self.inactive(name, &row)? { return Ok(None); }
        if !self.check_rule(name, Effect::Read, None, &row)? {
            self.hidden_row();
            return Ok(None);
        }
        // Memory deadlines are hidden by Disclosed itself; transfer the live
        // boundary before projecting, still inside this candidate's expiry scope.
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { self.note_expiry(table, &row); }
        let projection = self.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query();
        let visible = projection.into_value();
        Ok(Some(AuthorizedRow { projection: self.disclosed_row(table, visible).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: expiry.defer() }))
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_disclosed(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.scan_authorized(name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit,true,true,#[cfg(feature = "server-search")] None)
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_authorized(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool, trusted: bool, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields: Option<&[String]>) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.scan_authorized_prepaid(name, index, prefix, bounds, dir, limit, metered, trusted, false, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.7 — a delivered uniform prefix has prepaid its
    // logical rule once; provisional rows still undergo physical authorization.
    pub(super) fn scan_authorized_prepaid(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool, trusted: bool, rule_prepaid: bool, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields: Option<&[String]>) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // A public, non-expiring image is authorized before accessing storage.
        // No hidden candidate can influence its actual-size decoder admission.
        // Trusted selected heads may use the same actual-size adapter. It
        // decodes a full transient image; the streaming consumer retains only
        // its ordering key. Selection remains useful in the private decoder.
        if (self.context.is_system() || trusted && !table.read_lifetime_sensitive()) && table.recorded.is_empty() && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a| a.audience) {
            if metered { self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'_>>,Vec<u8>,
                )>(limit))?; }
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            let live = table.read_lifetime_sensitive();
            // A web device never reads as the system, the only reader of an expiring table here.
            #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
            let live = false;
            let rows = if live { self.live_store_scan(name, index, prefix, bounds, dir, limit, metered)? }
            else if metered {
                self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan_metered(scope,name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit,&mut runner.meter)
                })?

//! The services card. Quarry examples are compiled by tests/guide.rs.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Services: a worker's seat, and accounts the application authenticates

A service seat gives a worker its own principal for rule-checked operations.
A service can also mint sessions for accounts the application authenticates.

```quarry
table rankings:
  postId: id
  score: int
  unique postId
  read <- allow
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ranker'     -- a text literal beside viewer names a principal
  update <- viewer = 'service:ranker'

mutation rank(postId: id, score: int):
  upsert rankings[postId = postId] { postId, score }
```

An application that authenticates its own accounts — a phone number it
verified, a member of its own directory — mints their sessions through its
service and hands each token to that account's device, which then talks
to the server directly with `createLocalClient({ url, session })`:

```ts
import { mintSession, revokeSessions } from "snapback4/worker";
const session = await mintSession(url, serviceToken, "member:42", "member", 30);   // POST /auth/session
await revokeSessions(url, serviceToken, "member:42");                              // POST /auth/revoke
```

The principal is the application's own name for the account, outside the
engine's namespaces (`dev:`, `guest:`, `user:`, `service:` and `system`
are refused). `snapback4 dev` and `snapback4 serve` both take these
tokens; the adapter that owned every request becomes an identity provider
and a worker.
For this unbound application mint, an absent or non-string `kind` defaults to
`account`; an absent or non-integer `days` defaults to 7; integer days clamp to 1–3,650.

## Operations

`snapback4 token --service ranker [--days 365] [--release <dir>]` prints,
once, a bearer session for the principal `service:ranker`.
The default prints the token plus one newline on stdout and usage details on stderr.
Use `TOKEN=$(npx snapback4 token --service ranker --quiet)` for token-only output
with no successful stderr. `--json` instead prints one object containing `token`,
`principal`, `expiresAt` (milliseconds) and `store`, with no successful stderr;
--quiet and --json are mutually exclusive. Capture stdout separately from stderr.
The owning store is printed on stderr: the project's `.snapback4/store.sqlite`,
or `<dir>/store.sqlite` with `--release <dir>`. A token belongs only to that
store; mint one for each isolated audit or proof store after starting its dev
server. While dev or serve owns the store, the CLI asks that server to mint
through its loopback listener. Run it on the server host as the store owner;
`serve` uses `snapback4 token --service ranker --release <dir>`.
The server requires a loopback peer and mutual proof of the per-run secret in
the 0600 `<database>-token` file. The secret never crosses the wire; nonce
proofs bind the service, days and store, and the returned token is encrypted
for this exchange. Public listeners still refuse remote minting. Keep that file private. With the server stopped, the same command mints offline.
`--fresh` (also `--memory`) has no disk store for this CLI to mint into.
Readiness for dev and serve is the first stdout line: the listening URL,
emitted after store and native-module setup. Wait for that line before sending
requests; lifecycle logs and the store banner use stderr.
Declared `snapback/workers/<service>.mjs` entries receive their own service
token through the environment in both dev and serve (guide effects).
Automatically injected tokens are never printed; child logs redact them.
Rules name it
like any principal, so the ranker is admitted and nobody else is; with
its token a worker claims effects (guide effects) and runs any operation
a rule admits it to.
An audience read rule may add `or viewer = 'service:ranker'`; sync ignores
service grants, and `check --residency --verbose` lists them without changing any device's partition.

## Refusals

E_STORE_BUSY from `token` names the owner when its loopback listener or private
proof is unavailable. Restore local access and rerun token, or stop the named
server, mint offline, then restart it. An older server needs that offline path
or an upgrade. Another CLI owner must finish first. Leave `<database>-owner`
in place. A lost reply can leave an unused session until expiry; the CLI does
not automatically retry minting.

E_AUTH means the request has no live principal or uses the wrong authority
for its endpoint. For example, a user's bearer token cannot claim effects:
mint a service token with `snapback4 token --service pusher` against the
same store and supply it to the worker. Application requests use a live
user session; loopback `dev` may instead use `x-snapback-persona: alice`
for a declared persona (an undeclared name is refused, naming the `use personas`
line to extend). `serve` never accepts that persona header. Renew
or sign in again for an expired session, and check that the token belongs
to the backend the URL actually reaches.
Detailed dev explanations require a loopback peer, a loopback Host and, when present,
a loopback browser Origin, just like dev-only endpoints. An unknown token may come from
an earlier/replaced store or another instance, or have expired or been signed out,
including before this server started. Sign in again and discard saved tokens from an
earlier store. Expiry is identified when the session record remains; absent records
cannot distinguish expiry from sign-out or store replacement. No token history is kept.
SDK local/online clients and the app online arm preserve that message after terminal
refusal, including a device app restoring a saved foreign-store token and a later
local logout-everywhere call. Ordinary signed-out clients still ask you to sign in. Serve and untrusted dev
requests, including declared public POST routes, use identical text for every cause:
"this session is not live; sign in again", without disclosing whether a token was issued here.
Refused session tokens on data and identity doors (logout, narrow, refresh and logout-all)
use HTTP 401, E_AUTH, family `auth` and `guide: personas`, without an E_AUTH prefix
inside the message. No new field is needed. Do not branch on diagnostic prose.


E_OIDC_BINDING means begin did not receive a 43-character base64url SHA-256
binding hash. Hash the browser's fresh binding secret and supply that hash
to begin; keep the secret in that browser for completion. This is not the
provider client secret. Do not log either secret or substitute a fixed value.

//! Real upload ledgers for foreign-id and non-image placement challenges.
// @ref LLP 3000.038#3-upload — assets retain upload ownership and verified media type.
use super::*;
const PDF:&[u8]=b"%PDF-1.4\n%%EOF\n";
pub(super) fn file(e:&mut Engine,owner:&str,types:&[String])->Result<Value,String>{
    let mime=types.first().map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("application/pdf");
    let (bytes,name)=match mime{"application/pdf"=>(PDF,"proof.pdf"),"text/plain"=>(b"verified text\n".as_slice(),"proof.txt"),"image/png"=>(PNG.as_slice(),"proof.png"),_=>return Err(format!("file ledger needs a verified {mime} fixture"))};
    e.store_file(owner,bytes,Some(mime),name,NOW).map(|a|a.column_value()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())
}
fn contains(value:&Value,id:&Value)->bool {
    if value==id{return true;}
    match value{Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|v|contains(v,id)),Value::Map(m)=>m.values().any(|v|contains(v,id)),Value::Row(m)=>m.values().any(|v|contains(v,id)),_=>false}
}
pub(super) fn run(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    let Ok(initial)=readback(fixture)else{return;};
    if initial.values().any(|xs|!xs.is_empty()){
        out.unsupported.insert("foreign-asset replay over populated fixtures needs full ledger transfer; empty-ledger challenges run separately".into());
    }
    for bp in bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|p.program.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation&&!p.program.native){
        let p=&bp.program;
        for (arg,kind) in &p.args{
            if !matches!(kind,ir::ArgType::Image{..}|ir::ArgType::File{..}){continue;}
            for mode in ["foreign upload","non-image photo"]{
                if mode=="non-image photo"&&!matches!(kind,ir::ArgType::Image{..}){continue;}
                let mut e=match engine(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),program("$security.asset",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(error)=>{out.unsupported.insert(error);continue;}};
                let sample=if mode=="non-image photo"{file(&mut e,VIEWER,&[])}else{match kind{
                    ir::ArgType::Image{..}=>e.store_asset(OTHER,&PNG,NOW).map(|a|a.column_value()).map_err(|e|e.to_string()),
                    ir::ArgType::File{types,..}=>file(&mut e,OTHER,types),_=>unreachable!(),
                }};
                let sample=match sample{Ok(v)=>v,Err(error)=>{out.unsupported.insert(error);continue;}};
                let Some(mut args)=arguments(&p.args,0)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: asset challenge needs remaining argument fixtures",p.name));continue;};
                args.insert(arg.clone(),sample.clone());
                out.attempted+=1;*out.operations.entry(p.name.clone()).or_default()+=1;
                match e.mutate(&p.name,None,Context::new(VIEWER,args).at(NOW)){
                    Ok(result) if matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Failed{..})=>out.refused+=1,
                    Ok(_)=>{if let Ok(rows)=readback(&e)&&rows.values().flatten().any(|row|contains(row,&sample)){
                        out.boundary_failures.push(format!("{} placed {mode} through argument {arg}",p.name));
                    }},
                    Err(error)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: asset challenge engine error {error}",p.name));},
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
